## Stage A, correction C3

- **Session:** SV3 Stage A's correction C3, started by Nathan from [C3's prompt](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-dating-app/blob/8d83474a354603cee11b8a09fe66b63a3602074f/docs/ephemeral/2026-11-13-sv3-stage-a-c3-correction-prompt.md), revision 1, from `2e3ef0f7c327801e8052c1f7f92849b75440ed3f` of the manager branch `claude/magical-wozniak-yfmmx2`, on 13 November 2026.
- **Specification:** the [brief](../../planning/sv3-eligibility-discovery-interactions.md), revision 9, "Stage A, correction C3", with [DM-67](../../continuity/dev-manager/reviews/2026-11-13-dm-67-sv3-stage-a-c2-delta-review-dispositions.md) section 3.10's GD3 to GD10 for the words.
- **Branch:** `claude/glow-discovery-c3-ir9mnd` (pushes to `claude/**` run the Foundation workflow).
- **Commits,** all on `2e3ef0f`: `b7f70c0` (items 1 and 2: the lock scan, with its offline tests), `1332388` (items 3 and 4: `pages.one_snapshot_statements` and the list's matcher, with its offline tests), `72ace21` (item 5: the proof README, GD3 to GD7 and C3's sentence), `945e734` (GD8 and GD9 in this record), and this records commit (this section and GD10). **Code head:** `72ace21`.

### The start gate, the environment and the toolchain

- **Environment (names only; no value was read or printed):** none of `DATABASE_URL`, `HD_API_KEY`, `GEO_API_KEY`; no `STREAM_*` and no `PG*` name (checked again by the local database script before it started). The three AWS names were present (`AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `AWS_CA_BUNDLE`, `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`), never read and passed to no command; `GH_TOKEN` and `GITHUB_TOKEN` present, never read. Every repository command ran under `env -i` with `PATH`, `HOME`, `LANG=C.UTF-8` and its own variables (the proxy and CA variables by reference, for the package install only).
- **Toolchain:** `python3.12 --version` → `Python 3.12.14`; `docker --version` → `Docker version 29.9.2, build a82e528` (its daemon not running at the start; started for the local runs and stopped after).
- **Start gate:** `git switch -C claude/glow-discovery-c3-ir9mnd 2e3ef0f…`; `git rev-parse HEAD` → `2e3ef0f7c327801e8052c1f7f92849b75440ed3f`; `C2's code head` printed; the owned paths' diff between `2e3ef0f` and the live manager branch printed nothing (the live branch was at `2e3ef0f`, which is on it); the count of "Stage A, correction C3" headings in this record on the live branch → `0`.

### The five items

Each reversal is a temporary edit, run offline or against its case on the local throwaway database, then undone (`git checkout` of `pages.py`, or the saved C3 file copied back and compared with `cmp`); `git status` after each showed only C3's own changes.

| Item | What changed | Tests | Reversals: the temporary edit, and the failing line |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1. A generic lock with a join** (L1) | `account_lock_scan.py`'s generic branch no longer turns an `of=` into `FOR UPDATE OF …`: with a join, only `no_key=True` passes | `test_a_generic_lock_with_a_join_passes_only_with_no_key`: the review's helper, `model.objects.select_related('account').select_for_update(of=('self',)).filter(pk=pk).first()`, is refused as `generic`, `FOR UPDATE`, with its join; the same helper with `no_key=True` passes as `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` | the scan as at `303fdcc`: on the helper, `ok … (_lock, generic, joins 1): FOR UPDATE OF self`, `account locks found: 1; not FOR NO KEY UPDATE: 0`, exit 0; the test against it: `AssertionError: Lists differ: [('generic', 'FOR UPDATE OF self')] != [('generic', 'FOR UPDATE')]` |
| **2. The joins the scan does not see** (L2) | **Arguments:** a positional argument of a lookup method and any argument of an expression method count as a join unless a literal `Q(…)`/`F(…)` or a name every assignment of which in the function is one, whose paths are judged (a `Q`'s keyword names, its `(path, value)` tuples, its nested `Q`s, an `F`'s string); a path method's argument that is not a string literal, and a keyword of a path method other than `flat`/`named`, count as a join; `*` or `**` in any call of the chain counts as a join. A keyword's name is a path; its value is judged for every `Q` or `F` in it or in a name it uses (through that name's assignments), and is otherwise a value. **Methods:** `latest` and `earliest` are path methods; `extra()` is a join. **Variables:** the lock's queryset is followed back through every assignment of its receiver's variable, and forward through every name its chain is assigned to and every name assigned from those, transitively, by the enclosing function's node (`_read_functions`, `_group`). **Reverse relations:** `read_models` reads `AppAccount`'s own relation fields' reverse names (`related_query_name`, else `related_name`, else `appaccount`): `{"appaccount"}`; a path whose first segment is one follows a relation, alone or with more segments, and an `of=` entry ending in one is refused like an account foreign key. **SQL:** a string that takes `FOR UPDATE` passes only if it is a plain literal, not formatted with `%` or `.format()`/`.format_map()`, not joined (`+`, `+=`, `.join`), with no `{` or `}` in it, that names a table after `FROM` and after every `JOIN` itself (`names_its_tables`), none of them the account table; any other is refused. The docstring states the rules and GD7's limits | `test_the_joins_c2_did_not_see_fail_the_scan`, each refused as `FOR UPDATE`: the brief's ten forms (a `Q` held in a variable; an `F` held in a variable; a `Q` from a helper; `latest('account__created_at')`; `extra(tables=[…])`; a join added two variables after the lock; `User.objects.filter(appaccount=…)` locked `FOR UPDATE`; `FOR UPDATE` in SQL with `JOIN {table}` in an f-string, with `FROM {table}` in a `.format` string, and `' FOR UPDATE'` as a string of its own), and nine more (a path in a variable, `annotate`, `*` arguments, `Q(…) \| Q(…)` given positionally, a chain assigned before the lock, a `Q` inside a `Q` in a variable, SQL formatted with `%`, joined with `+`, and with a subquery after `FROM`); `test_values_paths_with_no_join_and_complete_sql_still_pass` (a keyword's plain value through two variables, a `Q` in a variable with no join and `latest('created_at')`, complete SQL naming two tables, `no_key=True` with a reverse path); `test_the_sql_rule_reads_each_from_and_join` (two texts pass, eight fail); `test_the_reverse_names_of_the_accounts_own_relations_are_read`; C2's tests unchanged and passing | the scan as at `303fdcc` on each planted form: every one of the brief's ten → `account locks found: 0; not FOR NO KEY UPDATE: 0`, exit 0 (of the nine more, it already refused the path in a variable, and the chain assigned before the lock as a generic lock with no join); C3's lock scan tests against it: `FAILED (failures=19, errors=2)`: the ten forms and seven of the nine more `AssertionError: 0 != 1` (the chain assigned before the lock `AssertionError: () is not true`), L1's `Lists differ`, and the reverse names and the SQL rule `AttributeError` (no `reverse`, no `names_its_tables`) |
| **3. Before the probe point, an explicit list** (S1) | `discovery_cases.listed`: `predicates.VIEWER` exactly, with the viewer's ID as its `viewer` parameter; or `OWN_ACCOUNT_READ`, one `SELECT` of the account table's columns (each may carry an alias, as `values_list` writes), `FROM` the account table alone, `WHERE` its `id` `= %s` and nothing else, optionally `ORDER BY` the key (`ASC`), `LIMIT 1`, and `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` or `FOR SHARE`, with no parenthesis anywhere, whose parameters are a list or tuple of exactly the viewer's ID. `pages.one_snapshot_statements` applies it to every statement reading a member table in the `outside` and `transaction` phases, and its detail line counts them | `PreProbeListTests`: the three account reads as the pages send them (seen locally: the lock, the `FOR SHARE` read, the versions read) pass with a tuple or a list; `VIEWER` passes for the viewer only; ten reads fail (another ID; the viewer and another; an `IN` list; a join; a subquery in the condition and one in the columns; every other account; another condition; `FOR UPDATE`; a named parameter) and `VIEWER` for another member fails. The case on the database: listed reads 2, 3 and 2 | **D**, a pre-probe read of other members' accounts (`SELECT state FROM glow_persistence_appaccount WHERE id <> %s` with the viewer's ID, in `create` before `before_selection`): `FAILED: a page with no cursor: before the probe point, only the statements on the list read members` (C2's case passes it: `statements per transaction: a page with no cursor 19, …`) · **`VIEWER` for another member** (an entry's candidate, in `continue_page` before `before_recheck`): `FAILED: a page with a cursor: before the probe point, only the statements on the list read members` (C2's case passes it) · the matcher as C2's rule: `FAILED (failures=8)` in `PreProbeListTests` |
| **4. A page by cursor writes nothing, in any phase** (S2) | the write check reads `probe.seen`'s every phase (`outside`, `transaction`, `candidates`, `committed`), and the case requires the `committed` phase to hold the photo reads, so the check is seen to read after the commit | the case on the database: no write in any phase of the page by cursor | **E**, a write after the cursor page's transaction (the batch's `state` updated to itself, between `run` and `_with_photos`): `FAILED: a page with a cursor writes nothing (1 writes)` (C2's case passes it) · C2's own reversal, a write inside the transaction, still fails: `FAILED: a page with a cursor writes nothing (1 writes)` |

The records** (R1, N2; L2's limits) | the README: GD3 (the heading), GD4 (the file table's lock-scan row), GD5 and GD6 (T3's rule and limit), GD7 (the lock scan's "Limits" entry), and a sentence on what C3 adds; this record: GD8 (T5's two rows) and GD9 (C2's item 3 row); the P11 plan: GD10 (C3's run of record), in the records commit after the run | each old passage found once before it was replaced | — |

### What the corrected lock scan finds

`python -m glow_ordering_proof.account_lock_scan`, over `services/api` (its tests, migrations and virtual environment left out) and the reference design, on the code head: **seven account locks, all `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`,** at C1's seven sites; **`select_for_update calls read: 52; locking a queryset that joins: 0`** (47 in `services/api`, 5 in the reference design); **`account locks found: 7; not FOR NO KEY UPDATE: 0`**, exit 0. It refuses no existing lock, and no string outside a docstring in the scanned files takes `FOR UPDATE`. The reverse names it reads are `{"appaccount"}`; the account foreign keys are DM-65's ten.

- **A page by cursor:** `tx.read_account_shared`'s `SELECT state FROM glow_persistence_appaccount WHERE id = %s FOR SHARE`; the versions read, `SELECT "glow_persistence_appaccount"."eligibility_version" AS "eligibility_version", … FROM "glow_persistence_appaccount" WHERE "glow_persistence_appaccount"."id" = %s ORDER BY "glow_persistence_appaccount"."id" ASC LIMIT 1`; and `VIEWER`: 3, all on the list.
- **Not judged** (no member table): `run`'s `SELECT pg_backend_pid(), now()` (the `outside` phase), the pointers' read, the clock and the batch reads. No page makes a pre-probe statement the list does not name, so the stop rule's clause was not met.

### Deviations, readings and observations

- **How the given rules were read where the words leave a choice** (each refuses at least what the words require):
  - a positional `Q(…) | Q(…)` (a combination, not "a literal `Q(…)`") counts as a join;
  - a keyword's value is judged for every `Q` or `F` anywhere in it and in every name it uses, through those names' assignments, as C2's scan walked a value; DM-67's words judge it only when it is or names one;
  - "every plain assignment" includes one through a conditional or boolean expression (`query = (… if lock else …)`, the reference design's `_lock`), and an annotated or walrus assignment;
  - an `of=` entry ending in a reverse name of `AppAccount`'s relation fields is refused, as one ending in an account foreign key is;
  - a `related_query_name` or `related_name` on `AppAccount` that is not a literal makes `read_models` raise, so the scan fails closed;
  - a `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` string that names the account table or does not name its tables is still listed as a passing lock, as before.
- **The list's account-read shape allows a column alias** (`AS "eligibility_version"`), which `values_list` writes; the select list is otherwise columns of the account table only, so no subquery, join or other condition can pass.
- **`pages.one_snapshot_statements` gains one expectation and a count:** the page by cursor's `committed` phase must hold the photo reads (so S2's check is seen to read after the commit), and the detail line gives the listed pre-probe reads per page. The case's name, checks and count are unchanged (40 cases).
- **The README's changes are in the code commit `72ace21`,** as C2's were, so the run of record covers them.
- **The new offline tests stand after `unittest.main()`,** beside the existing `AccountLockScanTests` (N5, carried to Stage B).
- **The code was pushed while the local profile and discovery runs were still going** (05:03 UTC); both then passed. Nothing was pushed while the run of record ran; the records commit `945e734` was committed locally and pushed with this section after it.
- **Observed, not changed (outside the brief's list and GD7's limits):** the scan does not read `dates()` and `datetimes()` (which take a field path), `complex_filter()` (which takes a `Q` or a dict of lookups) or `in_bulk(field_name=…)` as path or lookup methods; and the SQL rule accepts a view's name after `FROM`, which would lock the view's tables (no migration creates a view). None occurs in a locked queryset today. For the manager.

### The stop rule

No condition was met: every change is in C3's owned paths (the lock scan, `discovery_cases.py`, their offline tests, the proof README, this record and the P11 plan's runs-of-record sentence); no app code, predicate, selection, recheck, lock, writer or workflow changed (the reversals' edits of `pages.py` were undone each time); the corrected scan refuses no existing lock in `services/api` or the reference design; no page makes a pre-probe statement item 3's list does not name; every control of the ordering suite failed as designed; and the ordering suite on both subjects and the identity, profile and discovery suites passed on the corrected code, locally and in the run of record.

- Any provider, HDE, Stream, Railway or other external call; `proofs/stream-chat` (its CI job ran unchanged).

## Delta re-review of C3 (code head `72ace21` against `303fdcc`)

- **The session:** the delta code and security re-review, from [its prompt](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-dating-app/blob/8d83474a354603cee11b8a09fe66b63a3602074f/docs/ephemeral/2026-11-13-sv3-stage-a-c3-delta-review-prompt.md), revision 1, at `acfe3d2` (TypeSafe v8.2: Opus 5.5 at high; Nathan's pick not recorded). Relayed by Nathan on 13 November. It changed nothing in the repository, on GitHub or in Notion.
- **Gates:** the environment (names only), the start gate and the classification (`"full": true`, `behavior-or-empty`) passed. Python 3.12.14, Docker 29.9.2.
- **Verdict: changes required, narrowly.** No blocking finding; C3 changes no app behaviour. Every form the brief names is refused, and every database reversal the record names fails the case. But the lock scan still passes four join forms that lock the account row `FOR UPDATE` and that no stated limit covers (F1 to F4); a fifth gap, from C1, sits in the expression C3 rewrote (F5); the write check misses a write to a table not named `glow_…` (F6); and the SQL rule's completeness test holds only at the string's own expression, with views unstated (F7). No code in `services/api` uses any of these gaps today.

| # | Severity | Correction class | Where | What | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Should fix | Yes: an account lock `FOR UPDATE` missed beyond a stated limit | `account_lock_scan.py:532-542`, `:559-563` | A keyword's value that is a function taking a path as a string, directly or through a name (`filter(state=Lower("account__state"))`, `Coalesce("account__state", …)`), joins the account table; the scan reads only a `Q` or an `F` there, and GD7 states only an expression from outside the function. GD1's rule ("otherwise a value") as written | Planted, it passes; compiled on PostgreSQL 17.11 with Django 5.2.17, it joins `glow_persistence_appaccount` and ends in ` FOR UPDATE` |
| F2 | Should fix | Yes | `:324-342`, `:345-363`, `:382-390` | A locked queryset combined with `&` (`locked & Profile.objects.filter(account__state=…)`), assigned or inline, is not followed | Planted, it passes; it joins the account table and ends in ` FOR UPDATE` |

| F4 | Should fix | Yes | `_of`, `:586` | `_of` reads its entries with `_text`, which accepts an f-string: `of=(f"{name}",)` reads as `FOR UPDATE OF {}` and passes; with `name="account"`, Django locks `OF "glow_persistence_appaccount"`. C2's code, in a function C3 changed | Planted, it passes |

From C1, in the expression C3 rewrote for L1 | Planted, it passes as `generic FOR NO KEY UPDATE` |

C2's pattern, reached by C3's S2 | Database reversal: `UPDATE auth_user SET is_active = is_active WHERE id = -1` after the cursor page's transaction; the case passed, 40/40 |
| F7 | Should fix (records, or code) | Yes | `_complete` `:596-616`; `names_its_tables` `:619-627` | "Not joined, not formatted" is checked at the string literal's own expression only, so a string assigned to a name and then joined or formatted passes; a view over the account table after `FROM`, `JOIN` or a comma passes (the session's observation). Neither is stated | Planted, each passes |
| N1 | Nit | No | — | `dates()` and `datetimes()` cannot take the lock (PostgreSQL refuses `FOR UPDATE` with their `DISTINCT`); `in_bulk(field_name=)` takes a field of the model, never a path | `NotSupportedError`; `FieldDoesNotExist` |
| N2 | Nit | No | `test_discovery_offline.py`, `test_other_reads_fail` | The matcher's tests do not pin an `OR` condition, and `a_join` fails first at its alias; the code refuses both | A widened shape passed the tests (`OK`) |

- **The focus, in brief:** L1 holds (the helper refused; with `no_key=True` it passes; on C2's scan it passed), with F5 beside it. ### What the corrected lock scan finds

`python -m glow_ordering_proof.account_lock_scan`, over `services/api` (its tests, migrations and virtual environment left out) and the reference design, on the code head: **seven account locks, all `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`,** at C1's seven sites (`reference.py:122` and `glow_chat/contact.py:167`, generic; `glow_identity/age.py:87`, `revocation.py:95`, `glow_profiles/identity_verification.py:55`, `tx.py:169` and `:185`, orm); **`select_for_update calls read: 52; locking a queryset that joins: 0`** (47 in `services/api`, 5 in the reference design); **`account locks found: 7; not FOR NO KEY UPDATE: 0`**, exit 0. It refuses no existing lock, the same output as at `72ace21`.

### The statements a page by cursor sends, as the corrected write check reads them

Seen in a local run of `pages.one_snapshot_statements` (each statement's text, never a parameter), every phase of the page by cursor (`continue_page` and its photo reads after the commit), 26 statements: `SELECT pg_backend_pid(), now()` ×3 (the page's `run` and each photo's) — read; `SELECT state FROM glow_persistence_appaccount WHERE id = %s FOR SHARE` ×3 — read; the settings pointers' `SELECT id, policy_version, artifact_ref FROM glow_persistence_policyrevision WHERE name = %s FOR SHARE` ×5 and their versions' `SELECT "glow_persistence_settingversion"."id", …` ×5 — reads; `SELECT clock_timestamp()` ×2 — read; the batch read `SELECT "glow_persistence_recommendationbatch"."id", …` — read; the versions read `SELECT "glow_persistence_appaccount"."eligibility_version" AS "eligibility_version", …` — read; `VIEWER` and `RECHECK` (`WITH v AS ( SELECT a.id AS account_id, …`) — reads; the photo reads `SELECT "glow_persistence_mediaasset"."account_id" AS "account_id" …` ×2 and `SELECT "glow_persistence_mediaasset"."id", …` ×2 — reads. **None is a write,** so the stop rule's clause was not met. The page with no cursor's control, in its candidates phase: `SELECTION` and a batch read (reads), and `INSERT INTO "glow_persistence_recommendationbatch" …` and `INSERT INTO "glow_persistence_recommendationentry" …` (writes, as the control requires).

### C4's own probes (an adversarial check of the rules' words)

Before pushing, the session ran a multi-agent check (ten agents: five probing items 1, 2, 3 to 5, 6, and regressions against `72ace21`, each planting forms by the rules' words, not their examples, against C4's scan and C3's; five verifying each finding independently). About 580 forms were planted. It found:
- **Three forms the rules' words name that `871b46b` still passed** (and C3's scan passed): a combination assigned in place with the lock on its right (`rows |= Match.objects.select_for_update().filter(pk=pk)`); a lock on a name holding an account combination or conditional, judged only as a generic lock, so `no_key=rows is AppAccount` passed; and a model held in a variable whose name begins with an underscore (`_model.objects.select_for_update(no_key=_model is not AppAccount)`), never treated as a generic lock. Each is refused at `ce260fc`, with its test and reversals (items 2 and 5 above).
- **No regression in the scan:** every planted body in C3's tests and 94 invented variants of following through names: whatever C3's scan refuses, C4's refuses, and every lock C3 judged is judged the same way; the scan's output over the tree is identical.
- **One change in the write check against `72ace21`:** `SELECT truncate_log(…)`, `SELECT truncated_at FROM t` and the like, which C3's `WRITE` counted as writes through its open-ended `\bTRUNCATE`, are reads under item 6's words. One verifier classed it a regression, the other a limit; making them writes would narrow item 6's words, which the stop rule forbids, so it is recorded as a stated limit (a write inside a function a read calls, SC9) and reported for the manager.
- **Stated limits** (forms no rule names, each passing at both heads; the full list, with an example each, is in "Stage A, correction C5"), in the README's further "Limits" entry with a planted example each: a value reached through tuple unpacking or `+=`, or assigned outside the lock's function; an expression's path built from literals (`Lower("account__" + "state")`); a combination by method (`locked.union(…)`); a queryset held through tuple unpacking, a container, a closure or a bound method (`q = rows.filter`); a `Q` changed in place (`q.add(…)`); `complex_filter` called through the class or `getattr`; a generic lock's `no_key=<variable> is AppAccount` where the variable is not a model (`rows = model.objects`, `self`) or `AppAccount` is rebound, never true, so the lock is `FOR UPDATE`; several statements in one string (`SELECT 1; DROP TABLE t`); and a `SELECT` that changes state through a function (`nextval`, `pg_advisory_xact_lock`, `set_config`).

### Deviations, readings and observations

- **How the given rules were read where the words leave a choice** (each refuses at least what the words require, and refuses no existing lock):
  - item 1: a `*` or `**` argument to a capitalised call counts as a join too, since the scan cannot read what it holds; an attribute's last name is the call's name (`models.functions.Lower`);
  - item 2: a combination assigned in place (`&=`, `|=`, `^=`) is a combination held in a name, on either side of the lock; a receiver that starts from a name holding such an expression is judged per chain (the probes' second form); the forward following climbs from every read of a followed name, which reaches every call C3's maps reached;
  - item 3: a keyword argument (`filter_obj=`) is read as a positional one, and an `F` counts as a join ("any other argument");
  - item 5: a variable whose name begins with an underscore is a model held in a variable (the probes' third form); C1's test was `islower()`.
- **SC10's place:** after the docstring's first paragraph, read literally as its two-line title; the paragraph on what every account lock must be follows it. SC9's words stand as given; the probes' limits are a separate entry after them.
- **Item 6 and `SELECT truncate_…()`:** a change from `72ace21` that follows item 6's words (above), reported for the manager, not narrowed.
- **C4's probes widened two rules' reach beyond the brief's examples** (items 2 and 5), in a third code commit; the brief's own forms were all refused at `871b46b`.
- **The README's file table and T3 entries are unchanged;** neither claims more than the scan or the check does. (Not so for the file table and C1's sentence, which said the scan finds every explicit account lock: C4's delta re-review's F3, corrected in "Stage A, correction C5".)
- **The new offline tests stand after `unittest.main()`,** beside C3's (N5, carried to Stage B).
- **The code was pushed while the local discovery run on `ce260fc` was still going** (19:32 UTC); the scan's output over the tree and the offline tests on `ce260fc` were checked first. Nothing was pushed while the run of record ran.

### The stop rule

No condition was met: every change is in C4's owned paths (the lock scan, `discovery_cases.py`, their offline tests, the proof README, this record and the P11 plan's runs-of-record sentence); no app code, predicate, selection, recheck, lock, writer or workflow changed (the reversals' edits of `pages.py` were undone each time); the corrected scan refuses no existing lock in `services/api` or the reference design; the corrected write check counts no statement the real page by cursor sends as a write; every control of the ordering suite failed as designed; and the ordering suite on both subjects and the identity, profile and discovery suites passed on the corrected code, locally and in the run of record.

### What could not be run here

- The API's, the contracts' and the mobile app's checks, and `playwright install` (CI's; nothing under them changed).
- Any provider, HDE, Stream, Railway or other external call; `proofs/stream-chat` (its CI job ran unchanged).

### The six items

Each reversal or mutant is a temporary edit, run offline or against its case on the local throwaway database, then undone (the saved file copied back and compared with `cmp`, or `git checkout`); `git status` after each showed only C5's own changes. | Item | What changed | Tests | Reversals and mutants: the temporary edit, and the failing line |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1. A lock's receiver name, followed through every plain assignment** (F1; class 2) | `_receiver_heads`' guard is now `_reaches_a_composite`: from the receiver's name, through each plain assignment in the function (`=`, an annotated assignment, `:=`, a combination assigned in place, as `_read_functions` already records them), transitively, through each value that is a name or a chain of calls, attributes or subscripts starting from one (`_chain_base`), until a value holds a combination, a conditional, a boolean or an assignment expression, or a name is combined in place; then every chain each of the name's values holds is judged as the receiver, as C4 did at one hop. A value that only passes a name on (`passed_on`) has the chains its name reaches put before its own, so the chain reported is one the expression holds (`orm`), not the intermediate name. Each name is followed once per lock (`reached`), and chains alike in what `_judge` reads (their capitalised names, whether they start from a variable, and that variable) are kept once, in first order (`_distinct`), so the verdicts are those of the unmemoised rule (below). ### What the corrected lock scan finds

`python -m glow_ordering_proof.account_lock_scan`, over `services/api` (its tests, migrations and virtual environment left out) and the reference design, on the code head: **seven account locks, all `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`,** at C1's seven sites (`reference.py:122` and `glow_chat/contact.py:167`, generic; `glow_identity/age.py:87`, `revocation.py:95`, `glow_profiles/identity_verification.py:55`, `tx.py:169` and `:185`, orm); **`select_for_update calls read: 52; locking a queryset that joins: 0`**; **`account locks found: 7; not FOR NO KEY UPDATE: 0`**, exit 0. The output is byte-identical to the scan's at `ce260fc`. A per-call dump of all 52 calls (every chain each receiver holds, each chain's verdict, and the joins) is identical at `ce260fc`, `c5d9bc5`, `7f789a4` and `43986ba`, but for the form of each path. No verdict on the tree changed, so the stop rule's clause was not met.

### C5's own probes (an adversarial check of item 1 and item 5)

Before pushing, the session ran two rounds of multi-agent checks. **Round 1** (16 agents: four probers, twelve verifiers, against C5's first local commit `c5d9bc5`):
- **Item 1, by its words:** 24,401 forms (two to four hops through every kind of plain assignment, every kind of value, from every kind of expression, at six placements and three lock forms, and 47 hand-written ones). Every form item 1's words name is refused at C5, as `orm`, `FOR UPDATE`; `ce260fc` passes 12,188 of them as `generic`, `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`. No form the words name still passes.
- **Regressions:** 4,264 forms (every planted body in C2's to C5's tests and about 4,060 variants). No form refused at `ce260fc` passes at C5, and every lock's joins are identical.
- **The tree and the diff:** an independent AST census finds 52 calls and the seven account locks, all `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`; every call's verdict is the same at both heads; the diff changes only item 1's rule and the words that state it (the order of the chains reported is reporting, not a rule).
- **Mutants:** every mutant the review lists fails C5's pins; the prober's own variants of the same branches found the twelve further mutants listed above, which C5 then pinned.
- **Two shapes C5 refuses that are not account locks** (confirmed by two verifiers each; recorded as readings below), and **a performance defect** (a diamond of names, each re-assigned twice from the one before, took 5.5 s at 16 levels and over 60 s at 20, against 0.003 s at `ce260fc`), which the memoisation in `7f789a4` removed: 40 levels now take a fraction of a second, refused as `orm`.
- **The memoisation changes no verdict:** every form of round 1's corpora (28,786 forms) gives the same output at `c5d9bc5` and `7f789a4`, as do item 1's forms, the stated limits' examples and the tree.

**Round 2** (two probers, a fresh mutant prober and a memoisation prober, each finding checked by an independent verifier: 43 confirmed and 5 refused when this was written, two still running; against `7f789a4`):
- **The memoisation changes no verdict:** 9,847 forms (cycles, diamonds, re-used names, several locks sharing names, capitalised names and `AppAccount` mid-chain), the same `(kind, mode, joins)` at `c5d9bc5` and `7f789a4`, and, comparing each lock's raw chains in first order, no difference over 18,243 locks.
- **Performance:** the scan stayed exponential where chains differ only in capitalised attribute names (`x1 = x0.A`, `x1 = x0.B`, …: 3.5 s at 16 levels) and on names each assigned from every other. `43986ba` keys the chains on what `_judge` reads (whether `AppAccount` is among their names, whether any is capitalised, the variable and whether it is one), not on every capitalised name: the A/B diamond now takes 0.01 s at 40 levels, ten names each assigned from every other with a capitalised attribute 0.30 s (33.7 s before). What remains exponential is the number of simple paths among names each assigned from every other: 12 such names (132 assignments in one function) take about 2 s, 13 about 7 s. Nothing like it is on the tree; it is recorded as an observation.
- **Mutants:** 69 new ones; 48 passed `7f789a4`'s module while changing a verdict on some form. C5 pinned each that lets a lock through or changes the kind reported (the tests above), and records the rest ("Round 2's mutants" above).
- **The final key changes no verdict:** every form of both rounds' corpora (38,656 forms) and 6,000 random forms give the same output at `7f789a4` and `43986ba`; the random forms also show no form refused at `ce260fc` passing at C5, and no change of joins.

### Every stated limit the README holds, with its planted example

The lock scan's and the write check's stated limits (the README's "The lock scan", "A page by cursor's write check", "Further forms the lock scan and the write check do not see" and "Further forms C4's delta re-review found"). Each planted example was run through `scan_file` at C5's head (`43986ba`; also at `c5d9bc5` and `7f789a4`, the same) and at `ce260fc`, in a file of its own with the models imported, and passes at both: "unseen" is no lock listed; "passes" is a lock listed as `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`. The write check's were run through `discovery_cases.is_write`, which C5 does not change.

| Limit | Planted example (the lock's function) | At `43986ba` and `ce260fc` |
|---|---|---|
| The joins of a queryset from another function | `rows = joined()` (a helper returning `Match.objects.filter(account_low__state=...)`), then `rows.select_for_update(no_key=rows is AppAccount)` | passes (`generic`) |
| An expression given as a keyword's value from outside the function | `filter(display_name=expression)`, `expression` a parameter | unseen |
| A field path given to an expression through a name | `path = 'account__state'`; `filter(display_name=Lower(path))` | unseen |
| An expression class called by a lower-case name | `lower = Lower`; `filter(display_name=lower('account__state'))` | unseen |
| A model's `Meta.ordering` | `Profile.objects.select_for_update().first()` (the scan reads no `Meta`; none exists today) | unseen |
| A model imported under another name | `from glow_persistence.models import AppAccount as Account`; `Account.objects.select_for_update()…` | unseen |
| A queryset of `AppAccount` held in a capitalised variable | `Rows = AppAccount.objects.filter(pk=pk)`; `Rows.select_for_update()` | unseen |
| A lock clause built at run time | `cursor.execute(f'SELECT id FROM glow_persistence_appaccount WHERE id = %s {mode}', …)` | unseen |
| A view after `FROM`, `JOIN` or a comma | `'SELECT id FROM account_view WHERE id = %s FOR UPDATE'` | unseen |
| A string formatted after it is assigned to a name | `sql = 'SELECT id FROM glow_persistence_match WHERE id = %s FOR UPDATE'`; `cursor.execute(sql.replace('glow_persistence_match', table), …)` | unseen |
| A `bytes` string | `cursor.execute(b'SELECT id FROM glow_persistence_appaccount WHERE id = %s FOR UPDATE', …)` | unseen |
| `dates()`, `datetimes()`, `in_bulk(field_name=...)` (no rule needed) | `Profile.objects.select_for_update().dates('account__created_at', 'day')` | unseen |
| A value reached through tuple unpacking | `e, g = Lower('account__state'), 1`; `filter(display_name=e)` | unseen |
| … or `+=` | `expr = Upper(F('display_name'))`; `expr += Lower('account__state')`; `filter(display_name=expr)` | unseen |
| … or assigned in an enclosing function | `def outer(): expression = Lower('account__state')`, with `lock` inside using it | unseen |
| … at the module | `EXPRESSION = Lower('account__state')` at the module | unseen |
| … as a parameter's default | `def lock(pk, expression=Lower('account__state'))` | unseen |
| … as a `for` target | `for expression in (Lower('account__state'),):` | unseen |
| … as a `with` target | `with nullcontext(Lower('account__state')) as expression:` | unseen |
| … as an attribute | `self.expression = Lower('account__state')`; `filter(display_name=self.expression)` | unseen |
| An expression's path built from literals | `Lower('account__' + 'state')`; `Lower('account__%s' % 'state')`; `Lower('account__{}'.format('state'))`; `Lower('account__state' if flag else 'pk')` | unseen (each) |
| A queryset combined by method | `locked.union(Match.objects.filter(account_low__state=...))`; `.intersection(...)`; `.difference(...)` | unseen (each) |
| A queryset held through tuple unpacking | `locked, other = Match.objects.select_for_update().filter(pk=pk), None`; `locked.filter(account_low__state=...)` | unseen |
| … a container | `held = [Match.objects.select_for_update().filter(pk=pk)]`; `held[0].filter(account_low__state=...)` | unseen |
| … a closure | `def more(): return locked.filter(account_low__state=...)` inside the lock's function | unseen |
| … a bound method | `q = rows.filter`; `q(account_low__state=...)` | unseen |
| A `Q` changed in place | `q.add(Q(account_low__state=...), Q.AND)`; `q.children.append(('account_low__state', ...))` | unseen (each) |
| `complex_filter` through the class or `getattr` | `QuerySet.complex_filter(rows, {...})`; `getattr(rows, 'complex_filter')({...})` | unseen (each) |
| A generic lock's `no_key=<variable> is AppAccount` where the variable is a manager | `rows = model.objects`; `rows.select_for_update(no_key=rows is AppAccount)` | passes (`generic`) |
| … `self` | `self.select_for_update(no_key=self is AppAccount)` | passes (`generic`) |
| … `AppAccount` rebound | `AppAccount = None`; `model.objects.select_for_update(no_key=model is AppAccount)` | passes (`generic`) |
| … a plain account chain held in a name (C5: unchanged) | `c = AppAccount.objects.filter(pk=a)`; `d = c`; `d.select_for_update(no_key=d is AppAccount)` | passes (`generic`) |
| L1 | `filter(display_name=Case(When(account__state=1, then=2)))`; with `Q as Cond`: `Case(When(Cond(account__state=1), then=2))` | unseen (each) |
| L2 | `functions['Lower']('account__state')`; `getattr(functions, 'Lower')('account__state')`; `_Lower('account__state')` | unseen (each) |
| L3 | `filter(display_name=Window(RowNumber(), partition_by=['account__state']))` | unseen |
| L4 | `self.Model.objects.select_for_update()` (`Model = AppAccount` on the class); `{'a': AppAccount}['a'].objects.select_for_update()` | unseen (each) |
| L5 | `take = AppAccount.objects.select_for_update`; `take()`; `getattr(AppAccount.objects, 'select_for_update')()` | unseen (each) |
| L6 | `AppAccount.objects.filter(pk=pk).delete()`; `.update(id=new)` | unseen (each) |
| L7 | `c = Q(pk=pk)`, then `c, _ = {'account_low__state': 'active'}, 0`, or `for c in conditions:`, or `with ctx as c:`, before `complex_filter(c)` | unseen (each) |
| The write check: a write inside a function a read calls | `SELECT nextval(%s)`; `SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(1)`; `SELECT set_config(%s, %s, true)`; `SELECT truncate_x()`; `SELECT refresh_everything()` | read (each) |
| … several statements in one string | `SELECT 1; DROP TABLE t` | read |
| The write check's own reach (the observation) | an `UPDATE auth_user …` from a thread on its own connection inside `continue_page`'s transaction (item 4's row) | the case passes (local database, `c5d9bc5`'s code, with `pages.py` edited for the run; neither `pages.py` nor the write check changed after it) |

### Commands and results (on the code head `43986ba`)

**The proof** (`proofs/postgres-ordering`; `python3.12 -m venv .venv`, `.venv/bin/python -m pip install --require-hashes -r requirements-dev.lock`, `pip check` → `No broken requirements found.`): `env -i … python -m unittest discover -s tests -t .` → `Ran 296 tests`, `OK` (285 before C5: C5's eleven test methods; their forms are subtests); `ruff check .` → `All checks passed!`; `ruff format --check .` → `67 files already formatted`; `mypy` → `Success: no issues found in 67 source files`.

**The lock scan:** `python -m glow_ordering_proof.account_lock_scan` → seven `ok` lines, `select_for_update calls read: 52; locking a queryset that joins: 0`, `account locks found: 7; not FOR NO KEY UPDATE: 0`, exit 0; byte-identical to `ce260fc`'s output.

**Not run here:** the API's, the contracts' and the mobile app's checks (nothing under `services/api`, `packages/` or `apps/` changed; CI's, below).

### Local database runs (iteration, not evidence)

P06.DB's D4 conditions: a throwaway PostgreSQL 17.11 from the CI job's pinned image (`postgres@sha256:d74eeac9…ec46f`, pulled from Docker Hub after one `429 Too Many Requests` and a retry), started with a local `dockerd`, published on `127.0.0.1:5433` only, with `track_commit_timestamp=on`; a generated superuser and role password, a generated marker as each database's comment, and generated keys, in a 0700 directory under the session's scratchpad, written by a script that printed no value; none of `DATABASE_URL`, `HD_API_KEY`, `GEO_API_KEY` or any `PG*` name present (the script refuses otherwise). Each suite ran on its own fresh database after `migrate` from zero and `makemigrations --check --dry-run` (`No changes detected` each time); each later discovery run on a database re-created with a new marker. The container, its volume, the credential files and the saved logs were removed afterwards, and the daemon stopped.

- **The four suites on `c5d9bc5`'s code** (C5's first local commit: item 1 without the memoisation, and C5's first pins): **discovery:** `40/40 passed in 129.4 s` (`pages.one_snapshot_statements`: 18, 17 and 18, listed reads 2, 3 and 2; `locks.account_locks_no_key_update`: 7 explicit account locks, 52 calls read, 0 joining), both scans clean (`1363 secrets held; 0 found`, `747 member values held …; 0 found`), `DISCOVERY VERDICT: PASS`; **ordering**, both subjects (`--seed 20260929`): `[reference] cases: 56/56 passed`, `[adapter] cases: 115/115 passed`, `[read] cases: 62/62 passed in 19.1 s`, the delivery phase `checks 13/13 passed`, every control `failed as intended` (35 rows marked yes, none no), `== VERDICT: PASS ==`, in 499 s; **identity:** `49/49 passed in 36.8 s`, its scan clean, `IDENTITY VERDICT: PASS`; **profile:** `69/69 passed in 102.6 s`, both scans clean, `PROFILE VERDICT: PASS`.
- **Item 4's planted example and its contrast** (on `c5d9bc5`'s code, each on a re-created discovery database, `pages.py` edited for each run and restored): as in item 4's row.
- **The discovery suite on `7f789a4`'s code:** `40/40 passed in 129.3 s` (both lines as above), both scans clean, `DISCOVERY VERDICT: PASS`; **on the code head `43986ba`:** `40/40 passed in 120.0 s`, the lock scan's case as above, both scans clean, `DISCOVERY VERDICT: PASS`.

This session read the log for these lines only; it holds none of the job's values to search for.

### Deviations, readings and observations

- **How item 1's words were read where they leave a choice** (each refuses at least what the words require, and changes no verdict on the tree):
  - **The chain reported.** The brief has each of its forms "refused as `orm`, `FOR UPDATE`". C4 kept the receiver's own chain and the chains a followed value holds; C5 keeps every chain C4 judged (so no form refused at `ce260fc` can pass), and, for a value that only passes a name on, judges the chains its name reaches before the name's own, so the first failing chain, the one reported, is the account chain (`orm`) and not the intermediate name (`generic`). Where a form already followed at `ce260fc` holds such a value, the kind reported can change from `generic` to `orm`; no pass or refusal changes (round 1's regression corpus: 4,264 forms, no regression; kind changes only, from `generic` to `orm`, both `FOR UPDATE`).
  - **Two shapes refused that are not account locks.** Because the intermediate name is judged too, as C4 judged a followed value's own names, a lock passing `no_key=<name> is AppAccount` on a name that is not a model is refused where its name reaches a non-account combination through a hop: `c = Match.objects.filter(pk=a) | Match.objects.filter(pk=b)`, `d = c`, `d.select_for_update(no_key=d is AppAccount)` (and with `d = c.filter(...)`, `d = c[:5]`, three hops), and `model = Match if flag else Profile`, `rows = model.objects.filter(pk=pk)`, `rows.select_for_update(no_key=rows is AppAccount)`, each now `generic`, `FOR UPDATE` (passing at `ce260fc`). Each locks only `Match` or `Profile` rows, with a `no_key` that is always false: the README's stated-limit shape (a comparison that is false whenever the lock is on the real `AppAccount`). The refusal fails closed and touches no lock on the tree; without `no_key`, the same lock on a lower-case name was already refused at `ce260fc` as a generic lock (C1's rule). Not a correction class (it is no false pass, regression, test or record); recorded for the review and the Dev Manager.
  - **A chain of calls starting from a name** includes a call whose function is the name (`d = foo(c)` follows `foo`, not `c`), as `_heads` already read a chain; `d = list(c)` stays a stated limit (round 1: "a call wrapping `c` is not a chain starting from it").
- **The memoisation** (`reached`, `_distinct`, `_judged_alike`) is part of item 1's rule's implementation, not a rule: it was added after round 1 found the exponential time, and keyed on what `_judge` reads after round 2; it changes no verdict (the tree, both rounds' corpora, item 1's forms and the stated limits' examples, each identical at `c5d9bc5`, `7f789a4` and `43986ba`). It is part of "item 1's rule" for C5's review's bounded diff.
- **A remaining performance observation:** the following is exponential in the number of simple paths among names each assigned from every other in one function (12 such names, 132 assignments: about 2 s). No code on the tree comes near it.
- **Item 5's mutants** are this session's concrete forms of the review's categories ("the in-place `assigned` entry" read as both `_climb`'s and `_read_functions`'; only the latter was unpinned by C4's tests). Dropping `UPDATE` or `INTO`, `_climb`'s in-place entry, and the read's start's case were already caught by C4's tests; C5's pins catch the first three as well.
- **Item 4's planted example for the observation** needed a temporary edit of app code (`glow_discovery/pages.py`), as a reversal's does; it ran only on the local database and was undone and compared (`git diff --quiet` on the file).
- **"Every stated limit the README holds"** is read as the lock scan's and the write check's stated limits (the four "Limits" entries above), the termination rule's sense; the README's other "Limits" entries are limits of other suites.
- **The new offline tests stand after `unittest.main()`,** beside C3's and C4's (N5, carried to Stage B).
- **The README's commit precedes the code commit** (`fb9b946`, then `43986ba`), so the run of record runs on the code head; both were pushed together.

### The stop rule

No condition was met: every change is in C5's owned paths (the lock scan, its offline tests, the proof README, this record and the P11 plan's runs-of-record sentence); no app code, predicate, selection, recheck, lock, writer, workflow or `discovery_cases.py` changed (the planted example's and the mutants' edits were undone each time); the corrected scan refuses no existing lock in `services/api` or the reference design, and changes no verdict on the tree; every control of the ordering suite failed as designed; and the ordering suite on both subjects and the identity, profile and discovery suites passed on the corrected code, locally and in the run of record.

### What could not be run here

- The API's, the contracts' and the mobile app's checks, and `playwright install` (CI's; nothing under them changed).
- Any provider, HDE, Stream, Railway or other external call; `proofs/stream-chat` (its CI job ran unchanged).
- The ordering, identity and profile suites locally on `43986ba` itself (they ran on `c5d9bc5`; `43986ba` changes only the lock scan's following, which none of them runs, and C5's tests); the run of record ran all four on `43986ba`.

### The stop rule's clock clauses

- **No case holds a date the run's clock prints.** The one clock text that prints a date in the new shape is the `Expires` header, at the run's own date; the results JSON and the database print ISO dates, the log records and the writes none (T5's table). Every date a case holds is a birth date: `years_ago(n)` with `n` from 15 to 151, `adult_date()` (30 or 31 years back), `2008-02-29`, `2001-02-29` and the discovery population's (20 to 59 years back, not held), and one future date, `years_ago(-1)`, a year ahead. None is in the run's year, so neither new form (nor a one-digit day's inside a two-digit day's) can match the `Expires` date.
- **No value found in a clock text:** the profile and discovery suites' value scans reported clean in the local runs and in the run of record (below).

### Deviations, readings and what could not be run

- **Markup only:** F2's and BB9's Markdown backticks are the docstring's double backticks.
- **The headers' rule also enumerates the HTTP day and month names and anchors the whole line**, narrower than "an HTTP date with a two-digit day" read loosely; anything else counts.
- **C2's three assertions converted** (above): read as item 5's own words, for the manager to confirm.
- **Item 1's tree check as a test** asserts the counts and verdicts; the byte-for-byte comparison with `8d83474`'s output is this record's (`cmp`), since a literal output in a test would pin `services/api`'s line numbers.
- **One install command** expanded an unset, misspelt variable name (`AWS_CA_BUNDLE_NOT_USED`, empty) into `PIP_CERT`; no AWS value was read or passed, and the install used the proxy's CA bundle.
- **The container restart:** the session's machine restarted during the first local sitting; the interrupted profile run is not counted, and the profile and discovery suites ran again on a new container (above).

### Outcome items, acceptance checks and the section's items

The reversals were run on throwaway local databases (iteration, not evidence; "Local database runs"), each a temporary edit in a detached worktree of `a9a63a2` (or of the head where a case changed after it), with the named case run alone, then the edit undone (`git status --short` empty before the next). ### `activate_reciprocal` (D10 (a))

`glow_chat/contact.py` gains one method, `OrmContactPersistence.activate_reciprocal` (`29fad40`): `activate_match`'s body with one check added under its account locks. Its inner body against `activate_match`'s (`ast.unparse`, unified):

```
--- activate_match.body
+++ activate_reciprocal.body
@@ -15,2 +15,5 @@
         raise _Refused(chat.MATCH_EXISTS)
+    for actor_id, target_id in ((low_id, high_id), (high_id, low_id)):
+        if not DirectionalInteraction.objects.filter(actor_id=actor_id, target_id=target_id, state='liked').exists():
+            raise _Refused('not_reciprocal')
     self._contact_state_checks(low_id, high_id)
```

Outside the body, the method's only addition is its one import, `from glow_persistence.models import DirectionalInteraction`, inside the method (DM-73 section 6.2); its signature, return and `self._run(body, probe)` are `activate_match`'s. **By AST** (`tests/test_interactions_offline.py`, `ReciprocalWriterTests`): every one of the 32 functions and methods in `contact.py` at `main`'s `947576d` is `ast.dump`-identical at the head, and the module with `activate_reciprocal` taken out is identical to `main`'s (no import, constant or class attribute changed); the only new definition is `OrmContactPersistence.activate_reciprocal` (with its nested `body`); its body is `activate_match`'s with exactly one inserted statement, the `For` above, after both account locks and the existing pair's refusal and before the contact checks. The comparison runs two ways: against `git show <main's merge base>:services/api/glow_chat/contact.py` where `main`'s history is present (it skips in CI's shallow clone), and against the 32 definitions' and the module's digests pinned from `947576d` (Python 3.12), which runs in CI. Its reversal: one character of an existing writer's reason changed (`already_unmatched`) fails both (`definition='OrmContactPersistence.unmatch'`). `glow_chat`'s existing tests are unchanged and pass (the API suite, 620 tests); `glow_chat` imports nothing from `glow_discovery` (`test_glow_chat_imports_nothing_from_glow_discovery`), and `activate_reciprocal` is called only from `glow_discovery/interactions.py`'s like, after its recheck (`test_activate_reciprocal_is_called_only_from_the_like_after_its_recheck`). On the database: `match.activate_reciprocal_refuses_what_activate_match_refuses` (each of `not_a_member`, `blocked`, `match_exists`, `profile_unavailable` twice (a paused profile; a withdrawal, which SV2 makes hide the profile), `consent_not_current` (a withdrawn decision seeded as test data with the profile left visible, a state no SV2 writer leaves), `account_not_active` refused alike by both writers, nothing written; and `not_reciprocal` for a `liked` row missing either way).

### The contract (D13)

- **Four new closed DTOs** in `gapp-api-v1.schema.json`: `InteractionUndoIntent` (`operation: interaction_undo`, `meta`, `target_profile_id`), `InteractionUndoOutcome` (`kind: interaction_undo`, `target_profile_id`, `version`, `state: withdrawn`; D13's projection state), `InteractionUndoReceipt` (`outcome_code: withdrawn`, `object_ref`, `committed_version`; D13's outcome code) and `InteractionUndoCommandResult` (`kind: interaction_undo_command_result`, the receipt, `replayed`, `current_projection`: the undo outcome, an `InteractionOutcome` or null; internal, as `InteractionCommandResult` is). **The only touch to existing definitions:** the intent appended to `AppIntent`'s `oneOf` and the outcome to `AppResponse`'s `data`, as SV2's intents and Stage A's `CandidateCardPage` were; no existing DTO's own properties or enums change (`InteractionOutcome` with `state: withdrawn` and `CommandReceipt` with `withdrawn` stay invalid, corpus cases pin both). No `ApiError` code is added.
- **`openapi.json`:** the four as component references; `paths` stays empty.
- **The registry (`flows-v1.json`), F08 only:** `InteractionUndoIntent` as a request contract and `InteractionUndoOutcome` as a response contract (projected to `owner`), and three new transitions after the existing five: `undo` from `passed` to `withdrawn` (`owner`, policy `A05`, eligibility not required), `like` from `withdrawn` to `liked` and `pass` from `withdrawn` to `passed` (`owner`, eligibility required, no policy). Allowed and forbidden examples in `tests/test_transition_contract.py`, `test_sv3_undo_is_the_actors_own_and_additive`: the owner allowed; another member, a revoked session, staff and the system forbidden; `undo` without `A05` `policy_unresolved`; a stale version `stale_version`; `like` or `pass` from `withdrawn` with eligibility unknown or denied `forbidden`; `undo` from `liked`, `withdrawn` or `none`, and a pass from `liked`, `state_conflict`; F09's rematch (`unmatched` to `active`) `state_conflict`; the projection the owner's only. It pins F08's first five transitions and first request and response, and F07, F09, F10 and F13 unchanged. `test_sv2_registry_prerequisites_are_pinned` passes unchanged.
- **The corpus:** 73 cases (14 valid, 59 invalid), 836 in all, through Python and the generated validators.
- **The README:** a section, "SV3 Stage B: the interactions' contract".
- **Regeneration** (`npm run generate --prefix packages/contracts`, twice; the second changed nothing): `gapp-api-v1.ts`, `validators.js` and `validators.d.ts` changed; `gapp-dev-v1.ts` did not. The Mobile checks job's `npm run check` passes with them and no other change (691 tests).
- **The server and the contract agree:** every request DTO's parse (`glow_discovery.contract.parse`) accepts what the schema accepts and refuses what it refuses (`RequestTests`, 16 refusals for each intent and each DTO's own), and every projection and command result is its DTO (`ProjectionTests`); the suite checks every served body against the schema (`check_body("AppResponse", …)`).

### D14's record of the row locks the suites send

In each suite, installed before its first statement and checked before its verdict (`row_locks.py`; the rule in the README's "Limits"). On the run of record (43686623299):

| Suite | Statements (through Django / through the driver itself) | Connections, threads | Writer's row locks | Of them naming the account table (`FOR NO KEY UPDATE` / `FOR UPDATE OF` other tables only) | Refused | The database's own functions, views, rules | Line |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ordering | 544,767 (544,758 / 9) | 10, 5 | 104,403 | 44,841 (44,841 / 0) | 0 | 0, 0, 0 | `ROW LOCKS (D14): PASS`, then `== VERDICT: PASS ==` |
| identity | 4,412 (4,407 / 5) | 6, 3 | 141 | 127 (127 / 0) | 0 | 0, 0, 0 | `ROW LOCKS (D14): PASS`, `IDENTITY VERDICT: PASS` |
| profile | 78,038 (78,013 / 25) | 26, 5 | 7,606 | 2,061 (2,061 / 0) | 0 | 0, 0, 0 | `ROW LOCKS (D14): PASS`, `PROFILE VERDICT: PASS` |
| discovery | 148,909 (148,845 / 64) | 60, 3 | 12,953 | 3,728 (3,728 / 0) | 0 | 0, 0, 0 | `ROW LOCKS (D14): PASS`, `DISCOVERY VERDICT: PASS` |

No statement the code sent before Stage B fails the check, once item 5's two harness locks are taken `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (D14's stop clause did not hold).

**Its reversal** (local, all four suites, `glow_chat`'s `_lock` without `no_key`): the table in "Outcome items". **Item 2's forms:**

| Form | How it is seen | Planted example |
|---|---|---|
| psycopg's `stream` through Django's cursor | closed: psycopg's `Cursor.stream` is wrapped in the suite's process; recorded as sent through the driver itself, and judged | `row_locks.driver_forms_are_seen` (`SELECT 1 AS planted_stream`, seen once as `driver`); reversal 38a (inside a page: the case fails); reversal 39 (an account lock `FOR UPDATE` by `stream`: the check fails) |
| psycopg's `copy` through Django's cursor | closed: `Cursor.copy` wrapped | the forms' case (`COPY (SELECT 1 AS planted_copy) TO STDOUT`, seen as `driver`) |
| `callproc` (DM-73) | closed: Django's cursor class sends it through the driver cursor's `execute`, which is wrapped | the forms' case (`SELECT * FROM "pg_backend_pid"()`, a literal argument written `%s`); reversal 38b |
| the driver's own connection (`connection.connection`) | closed: its `cursor()` makes a cursor of Django's class, a subclass of psycopg's `Cursor`, and its `execute` uses one; both wrapped | the forms' case (`SELECT 1 AS planted_driver_cursor`, `… planted_driver_execute`); reversal 38c |
| a lock taken inside a database function | closed by a rule and a check: a statement that creates a function or a procedure fails whatever its lock (B-C3; DM-77), and no function, procedure, view or rule exists when the check runs, outside `pg_catalog`, `information_schema`, the TOAST schemas and extensions, temporary schemas included since B-C3 (`row_locks.OWN_OBJECTS`), each zero, or the suite fails | reversal 40 (`CREATE FUNCTION planted_lock()`: the case fails, and the check counts `functions 1`) |
| libpq's own connection object (`connection.connection.pgconn.exec_`) | **a stated limit**: below psycopg's cursors | planted inside the page by cursor's transaction (`UPDATE auth_user …`): `pages.one_snapshot_statements` passes; in the forms' case, an account lock `FOR UPDATE`: the check passes |
| an asynchronous connection (`psycopg.AsyncConnection`) | **a stated limit**: its cursors are not psycopg's `Cursor` | planted from a thread inside that transaction with `asyncio.run`: the case passes |

Nothing is refused, so Django's own use of the driver's connection (creating cursors, the health check's `SELECT 1`, configuring a new connection) is untouched (DM-71's third condition): it is recorded and judged, and each suite's few statements through the driver itself are those (5 in the identity suite, 25 in the profile suite, about 60 in the discovery suite, 9 in the ordering suite, locally).

### Item 5: the ordering harness's two account locks (DM-73; EE1, EE6, EE8)

- **`planted.py`'s `_suspend`**, in the planted control `oracle.send_after_account_revocation`: `m.AppAccount.objects.select_for_update().get(pk=account)` → `m.AppAccount.objects.select_for_update(no_key=True).get(pk=account)` (Django sends `… FOR UPDATE` → `… FOR NO KEY UPDATE`).
- **`cases.py`'s `_harness_holder`**, in the named cases `f2_session_account_changed_under_wait` and `f2_match_pair_changed_under_wait`: `SELECT id FROM glow_persistence_appaccount WHERE id = %s FOR UPDATE` → `… FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, its docstring with it ("it locks one account row FOR NO KEY UPDATE (the app's account lock mode, DM-61, which a held no-key lock still blocks; SV3 Stage B's item 5, DM-73)").
- **The suite's design:** the two named cases are P06.2 B1's, which run on the adapter subject only (`cases.all_cases`: "the adapter (d5) is the only subject that carries it"; the reference design has no activation of its own), so each appears once per run; the control runs on both subjects. On the subjects where each runs:

| Line | `main`'s push run 43673382167 (on `947576d`) | The run of record 43686623299 (on `5d58993`) |
|---|---|---|
| `named.f2_session_account_changed_under_wait` (adapter) | `PASS`; `pid 133 wait_event_type=Lock wait_event=transactionid pg_locks not granted: transactionid/ShareLock pg_blocking_pids=[132] (holder pid 132) after 2 polls, 3 ms`; the harness transaction that moved the session: applied; the send that waited on the account lock: `refused:session_changed` | `PASS`; `pid 135 wait_event_type=Lock wait_event=transactionid pg_locks not granted: transactionid/ShareLock pg_blocking_pids=[134] (holder pid 134) after 2 polls, 3 ms`; applied; `refused:session_changed` |
| `named.f2_match_pair_changed_under_wait` (adapter) | `PASS`; `pid 133 … transactionid/ShareLock pg_blocking_pids=[132] (holder pid 132) after 2 polls, 3 ms`; the harness transaction that re-paired the match: applied; the unmatch that waited on the account lock: `refused:match_changed` | `PASS`; `pid 135 … transactionid/ShareLock pg_blocking_pids=[134] (holder pid 134) after 2 polls, 3 ms`; applied; `refused:match_changed` |
| `oracle.send_after_account_revocation` (reference) | `planted … declared oracle O4: flagged as intended`; `oracle: 1 violation(s) (O4)` | the same |
| `oracle.send_after_account_revocation` (adapter) | `planted … declared oracle O4: flagged as intended`; `oracle: 1 violation(s) (O4)` | the same |

Each arriver's wait is observed on the harness's backend, as before (a row lock's waiter waits on its holder's transaction), each verdict and outcome is unchanged, and the control still fails as intended on both subjects: item 5's stop condition did not hold. (A local ordering run before and after the change showed the same.)

### The threat model's inputs

**Every route, who may call it, and its refusals** (`glow_discovery/interaction_views.py`, `interaction_urls.py`; served only by the proof's discovery URL configuration). Each takes its actor from SV1's `GlowActorAuthentication` only: an `active` account with a `valid`, unexpired session at the current epoch; never from the body or another header. Every answer is the contract's (`AppResponse`, or `Error` naming a code and never a value), served `Cache-Control: no-store`; a refusal is logged by its internal reason code only.

| Route | Request → served data | Refusals |
|---|---|---|
| `POST api/v1/discovery/interaction` | `InteractionIntent` → `InteractionOutcome` (F08; F09's match) | no or bad token 401; another method 405; a query string, another content type, a body not its DTO 400; the actor failing a predicate 403; a stale, wrong, missing or consumed batch, a target outside it, an ineligible or consumed target, the actor as target, an expected version not the row's 409 `stale_version` (one body whatever the cause); a pass over the actor's own like 409 `state_conflict`; the same key with another intent 409 `idempotency_conflict` |
| `POST api/v1/discovery/interaction/undo` | `InteractionUndoIntent` → `InteractionUndoOutcome` (F08's `undo`) | as above for the token, method, query, type and body; undo off 403; an expected version not the row's 409 `stale_version`; no pass, not the most recent pass, or past `waiting_days` 409 `state_conflict`; the same key with another intent 409 `idempotency_conflict` |
| `POST api/v1/matches/unmatch` | `UnmatchIntent` → `MatchProjection` (F10) | as above; another member's match, or none, 404 `not_found` (before any lock, from the pair's identifiers); an expected version not the match's 409 `stale_version` |
| `GET api/v1/matches/<match_id>` | `MatchProjection` (F09) | no or bad token 401; another method 405; a query string 400; another member's match, or none, 404 `not_found` |
| `POST api/v1/blocks` | `BlockIntent` → `BlockOutcome` (F13) | as above for the token, method, query, type and body; the actor as target 409; a target with no profile 404; an expected version not the directional block's 409 `stale_version`; a block already active with no match to restrict, or an unblock with no active block, 409 `state_conflict` (`glow_chat`'s own refusals) |

Tests: `routes.interaction_refusals_and_ownership` (every route: 401, 405, a query; each `POST` route also another content type and a body not its DTO; another member's match, and an entry of another member's batch; a stale version on unmatch, block and interaction; **corrected in B-C2:** at Stage B this line said every route refuses a type and a body, but on each `POST` route only a body that is not its DTO, any query string or another content type is `invalid_request` (400), and on every route another method is `invalid_request` (405); the two `GET` routes refuse a query string (400) and read no content type; `GET api/v1/interactions/<profile_id>` refuses a body (400), and `GET api/v1/matches/<match_id>` reads none; the batch check targeted that batch's owner, not an entry in it; the exact-head review's #7 and #15, DM-75), `interactions.stale_and_ineligible_are_refused`, `interactions.pass_over_like_is_refused`, `settings.undo_off_and_on`, `unmatch.revokes_contact_and_never_rematches`; offline, `test_interactions_offline`'s request and route-log tests.

**Each action's recheck at commit: its locks, their order, and what it reads** (`glow_discovery/interactions.py`).
- **Order, every command:** the target's or the match's identifiers only before the locks (a profile's account, a match's pair: neither ever changes); then both accounts' rows `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, the lower first, each through SV2's `lock_account` (the actor's must be `active`; the other's is held in any state and judged after); then the settings' pointers `FOR SHARE` in one `read_shared` call, in name order (the like: the selection's five; undo: `discovery.undo`; unmatch and block: none); then the time from `clock_timestamp()`; then the idempotency decision (its record `FOR UPDATE`); `glow_chat`'s writers nest as savepoints after these locks, taking the same account order (DM-73 section 6.2).
- **Like and pass** read under the locks: the actor's own eligibility (`predicates.VIEWER`), the directional row and its version, the named batch (its owner, collection, state, expiry, the actor's three versions, the policy version) and its entry for the target, then the pair through the trusted adapter (`OrmPairActionTransaction`, `predicates.PAIR`: both accounts, age states, consents, profiles, the four fields and "show me" sets both ways, the age ranges, the distance, both block directions) and D7's consumption (`predicates.UNCONSUMED`, the same text the selection and the recheck apply). Every writer of a fact these read takes its owner's account row first, so nothing can change before the commit (D2). Tests: `concurrency.changes_during_a_like_are_seen` (DB10: a block, an unblock, a pause, a consent withdrawal, a preference change and a settings publication, each held at its commit while the like waits, each seen), and its reversals (the recheck removed, or read before the locks).
- **The match** (`activate_reciprocal`): the two account rows again (already held), then the accounts' states, an active block either way, an existing pair, the two `liked` rows, the profiles and consents; all under the locks.
- **Undo:** the setting, the directional row, the actor's most recent pass by `decided_at`, the waiting period against the transaction's clock.
- **Unmatch, block, unblock:** the match or the directional block and its version; `glow_chat`'s writers then lock the match or the block rows themselves.

**The match's uniqueness.** Two reciprocal likes lock the same two account rows in the same order, so one waits for the other's commit; the later one finds the earlier one's `liked` row and makes the match, the earlier one found none and returns `liked` with no match. The backstop is the canonical pair (`match_order`: `account_low < account_high`; `match_pair`: unique), so a second row is impossible. A pair with a match row in any state is consumed, so no later like reaches `activate_reciprocal`, and its `MATCH_EXISTS` refusal is a second guard. Tests: `concurrency.reciprocal_likes_at_once` (DB05: both arrival orders from separate connections, the second observed waiting on the first; one match, one `match_activated` event; fresh keys at once give one row), `match.reciprocal_likes_make_one_match`, `constraints.interaction_writes_are_refused` (`match_pair`, `match_order` refused by PostgreSQL).

**What each member can learn of the other's actions.**
- **Can:** that a like was reciprocated (the match, to both participants); that a match is `restricted` or `unmatched` (the same to both); the other member's card while they are eligible and unconsumed.
- **Cannot:** an unreciprocated like (nothing of the target's changes: its batch's state and version, the liker's card and every response to it are the same; `interactions.unreciprocated_like_is_hidden`); a pass; a block's direction from the block's own answers (the blocked member's own block answers as any first block does, and a block bumps only its actor's `blocks_version`; `block.works_after_unmatch_and_while_paused`), though a match only a block restricts, read by both participants, lets the member who did not block infer it (corrected in B-C2: the exact-head review's #1, D8's gap, carried to SV4 before P11; DM-75); why a like was refused (every stale cause answers the same body; `interactions.stale_and_ineligible_are_refused`); another member's match (404); a count. A refused like writes nothing.

**Idempotency.** Unique by actor, operation (`interaction`, `interaction_undo`, `unmatch`, `block`) and key (`intent_dedup`), the stored digest SV2's keyed digest of the whole canonical intent but its key (`salted_hmac` under the run's `SECRET_KEY`), checked after the locks and before anything stale: an identical repeat answers the current projection and, internally, the original receipt with `replayed: true`, even after its batch was consumed; the same key with another action, target, batch or expected version is `idempotency_conflict`; only a committed command stores its record, with its outcome code (`liked`, `passed`, `withdrawn`, `unmatched`, `blocked`, `unblocked`), object and committed version. Tests: `interactions.like_and_pass_are_idempotent`, `block.works_after_unmatch_and_while_paused` (the same key, the other action), `settings.undo_off_and_on` (an identical undo replays).

**Each setting's gate** (`discovery.*`, all off at launch).
- **Resurfacing:** off, a pass excludes for good; on, a pass decided at least `waiting_days` before the transaction's clock excludes no longer (`resurfaced_before`, NULL while off), in the selection, every recheck and the like's own check. Test: `settings.resurfacing_off_and_on` (**corrected in B-C2:** at Stage B it read first pages only, so the cursor's recheck was not reached; from B-C2 it reaches a resurfaced candidate on a page fetched by cursor; the exact-head review's #16, DM-75).
- **Undo:** off, `forbidden`; on, the most recent pass within `waiting_days`. Test: `settings.undo_off_and_on`.
- **Rematch:** not built; a publication with `enabled` on is refused naming `rematch_not_built` (with `rematch_never_after_block_or_report` when a block or report is allowed too) and writes nothing. Tests: `settings.rematch_cannot_be_switched_on`; offline, `test_interactions_offline.RematchTests`.

### Deviations, readings and what could not be run

- **The branch.** The session's own instructions named a different development branch; the prompt names `claude/brave-brown-o90q6i`, the stopped session's, and its start gate checks it, so the work is on it, and the push went there (the workflow runs on `claude/**`).
- **The Docker daemon** was not running in the container; it was started for the local database runs (D4's conditions otherwise as written). Docker Hub was reached only to pull the pinned image.
- **D14's reach closed by recording, not by a guard.** Item 2 allows a proof-only guard that refuses `callproc` and the driver cursor's pass-through methods; the record instead wraps psycopg's own `Cursor.execute`, `executemany`, `stream` and `copy` and `ServerCursor.execute` in the suite's process and records and judges what they send outside Django's `execute`, so those forms are seen, nothing is refused, and Django's own use of the driver's connection is untouched (DM-71's first and third conditions hold; the second, a refusal, does not arise). Each form is planted with its reversal; what remains (libpq's own connection object, an asynchronous connection) is a stated limit with its planted example. The reviewer may prefer a guard; the record's code is `row_locks.py` alone.
- **The database's own views and rules** are counted with its functions: a view or a rule could also take a lock out of a statement's sight. Each is zero.
- **Two reversal findings, fixed before the push:** `match.no_route_creates_a_match` did not catch a match-creating route at another path (15b), and now pins the exact routes served; the unmatch case did not catch `unmatched` dropped from consumption (31), because the pair's own `liked` rows also exclude it, and now also checks the unmatched match by itself (with the pair's directional rows removed as test data).
- **The F2 named cases run on the adapter subject only** (P06.2 B1's design); item 5's comparison is on the subjects where each case runs, and the control on both.
- **DB10's settings publication** (a new onboarding consent version) is answered `forbidden`, not the stale response: the actor's own acceptance fails CONSENT too, so the like refuses its viewer first (D3), having waited on the publication's pointer.
- **A withdrawn onboarding consent through SV2's route hides the profile**, so `glow_chat`'s `consent_not_current` refusal is reached in `activate_reciprocal`'s case only through a withdrawn decision seeded as test data with the profile left visible.
- **An earlier test my first install broke:** `test_p06_2_suite.UsedDatabaseTests.test_run_refuses_before_it_writes_anything` calls the ordering suite's `run` without Django set up, and the record's first install read the app registry; the record now reads the models' tables lazily and tolerates unconfigured settings, and `run` takes it out when it ends. The test is unchanged and passes.
- **DB04 for Stage B's tables** is a case the prompt does not list by name (`constraints.interaction_writes_are_refused`); it evidences the P11 plan's DB04 for migration 0011, as Stage A's batch case did for 0010.
- **The contract's union additions:** `InteractionUndoIntent` joins `AppIntent` and `InteractionUndoOutcome` joins `AppResponse`, as SV2's intents and Stage A's `CandidateCardPage` did; no existing DTO's own properties or enums change, and no registry entry changes but F08's lists gaining the two DTOs and its three new transitions.
- **The AST comparison with `main`** runs against pinned digests in CI, since CI's checkout has no history; the git form runs where `main`'s history is present.
- **D14's counts of threads and connections** are of distinct identities, which a closed connection's or an ended thread's successor may reuse (so "3 threads" in a suite whose cases start more).
- **Not run here:** the mobile app's `check:expo`, export and rendered checks (CI's Mobile checks job runs them); nothing else.

## PR47 and Codex on its head (`622a4f4`)

App Manager 11, 15 November 2026. [PR47](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-dating-app/pull/47) was opened at 13:16 UTC from the manager branch at `622a4f4`, whose non-Markdown tree equals Stage B's code head `5d58993`. Its push run 43718074254 and pull-request run [43718124052](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-dating-app/actions/runs/43718124052) on `622a4f4` each passed all eight jobs; the pull-request run's gate printed `Application checks passed`, with ordering 56/56, 115/115 and 62/62, identity 49/49, profile 69/69 and discovery 59/59 (its phase 181 s), each suite's D14 record `PASS`.

- **Codex's code review of `622a4f4`** completed with one finding, P2, on `services/api/glow_discovery/interactions.py` lines 307 to 308 ([thread](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-dating-app/pull/47#discussion_r4578551630)): with resurfacing on, a like or pass on a passed candidate who resurfaces must carry the actor's directional row's version, and no read gives a client that version (the card carries none, no route reads the row, and the stale response carries none); the proof's `interaction_cases._version()` reads it from the ORM.
- **The manager's check:** confirmed at `5d58993`. A client has the version only from its own command's answer (`InteractionOutcome`, or undo's outcome). The same holds after an undo (`withdrawn`, version above zero), and for a block after an unblock (`removed`; `interactions.py` line 518), which Codex did not raise; unmatch is not affected (the match read returns its version). It arises only with resurfacing or undo on (off at launch), or across devices for a block. The brief's D13 provides no such read (a design omission, approved in DM-60).
- **Disposition:** to Dev Manager 5 as [DM-74](../../ephemeral/2026-11-15-dm-74-sv3-stage-b-codex-version-read.md), before the exact-head review, since a fix changes the code the review reads. The review prompt is drafted and held until the answer. The thread stays open until the disposition is applied.
