- **The environment, by name only:** none of `DATABASE_URL`, `HD_API_KEY` or `GEO_API_KEY`; no `STREAM_*` or `PG*` name; the three AWS names present (`AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `AWS_CA_BUNDLE`, `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`), never read; `GH_TOKEN` and `GITHUB_TOKEN` present, never read.
- **The toolchain:** `Python 3.12.14`, Node `v24.19.0`, npm `11.9.0` (from `$HOME/.local/bin`), `Docker version 29.9.2`. Every repository command ran under `env -i PATH="$PATH" HOME="$HOME" LANG=C.UTF-8` with only its own variables (`GLOW_ENV=test` for the API).
- **Read:** the root `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, the three READMEs, `apps/mobile/AGENTS.md`; the brief's D8 to D15, "Stage B: interactions" and "Stage B, correction B-C1"; DM-74 whole; this record's "Stage B (interactions)", "Manager verification of Stage B" and "PR47 and Codex on its head"; Codex's thread on PR47 (one P2 comment on `interactions.py` lines 307 to 308, read with the GitHub tools); the contract files and tests the prompt names; the code the prompt names.

### The read (item 1)

- **The route:** `GET api/v1/interactions/<uuid:profile_id>` (`interaction_urls.py`, appended after Stage B's five; `interaction_views.OwnVersionsView`), for the actor only through SV1's `GlowActorAuthentication`, served `Cache-Control: no-store` through `_Route._answer`. Only `GET` (another method `invalid_request`, 405); a query string or a body `invalid_request` (400), as Stage B's routes refuse them; no new log call (the route's refusals log the existing `interaction route refused: invalid_request`).
- **The transaction:** `interactions.read_own_versions`, one `glow_profiles.tx.run` transaction whose first statement after the run's own `SELECT pg_backend_pid(), now()` is SV2's `read_account_shared` (the actor's account row `FOR SHARE`, as `read_match`), then `_account_of_profile` (the profile's account ID only), then the actor's own directional row (`DirectionalInteraction` with `actor_id` the actor, `target_id` the profile's account) and the actor's own block (`Block`, the same keys), each `values_list("state", "version")`. No lock site is added (the lock scan's output is unchanged), no setting is read (D19's check passes unchanged), and the function raises nothing of its own: the only refusal reachable is `read_account_shared`'s, for the actor's own account.
- **The DTO:** D13's new `OwnInteractionVersions` (`contract.own_versions`): `kind: own_interaction_versions`, `target_profile_id` (the profile ID as asked), `interaction_state` (`none`, `liked`, `passed`, `withdrawn`), `interaction_version`, `block_state` (`none`, `active`, `removed`), `block_version`; `none` with `0` where the actor has no row. No request DTO (the profile ID is the path's), no `ApiError` code.
- **Its answers,** on the throwaway database (iteration; the same cases ran in CI, "The run of record"):
  - after each command (`own_versions.each_state_and_version`): before any, `none 0 none 0`; a like `liked 1`; a pass `passed 1`; an undo `withdrawn 2`; a resurfaced like `liked 2`; a block `liked 1 active 1`; an unblock `liked 1 removed 2`; each equal to the command's own answer;
  - **the alike cases** (`own_versions.alike_wherever_the_actor_has_no_row`): a visible profile with no row, a missing profile ID, not visible (incomplete), paused, restricted (staff), removed (test data, as Stage A's case writes it), excluded (its "show me" set leaves the actor out, checked absent from the actor's pages) and blocking the actor. The case compares the status, `Content-Type`, `Cache-Control` and `Content-Length` and the body's bytes, with only the request's own `request_id` and the profile ID as asked replaced; all eight equal. Two of them as captured:

    ```
    visible, no row 200 Content-Type: application/json | Cache-Control: no-store | Content-Length: 278 {"contract_version":"gapp-api-v1","request_id":"<request>","data":{"kind":"own_interaction_versions","target_profile_id":"<profile>","interaction_state":"none","interaction_version":0,"block_state":"none","block_version":0}}
    missing 200 Content-Type: application/json | Cache-Control: no-store | Content-Length: 278 {"contract_version":"gapp-api-v1","request_id":"<request>","data":{"kind":"own_interaction_versions","target_profile_id":"<profile>","interaction_state":"none","interaction_version":0,"block_state":"none","block_version":0}}
    ```
  - **the other member's rows** (`own_versions.never_the_other_members_rows`): their like, pass and block of the actor each read `none 0 none 0`; the actor's own pass of the liker reads `passed 1`, and the actor's own block of the blocker `none 0 active 1`; each other member reads their own row;
  - **owner-only** (`own_versions.owner_only_and_held_for_share`): no token 401 `unauthenticated`, `POST` and `DELETE` 405, a query and a body 400, each `no-store`; the answer `no-store`; another member reads their own (`none`). While the actor's like holds the actor's account row at its commit (`before_commit`), from a separate connection, the read is observed waiting on it, then answers `liked 1`.
- **Recovery from no state,** each from a second device (the same member signed in again through SV1's route; it holds none of the first device's answers): with resurfacing on, a like of a passed candidate who resurfaces (`passed 1` read; `0` refused `stale_version`, nothing written; `1` applies, `liked 2`); with undo on, a like after a withdrawn pass (`withdrawn 2`; `0` refused; `2` applies, `liked 3`); and a block after an unblock, at launch (`removed 2`; `0` refused; `2` applies, `active 3`).

### The proof (item 2)

- **`_version()` before:** `row = _row(actor, target); return 0 if row is None else int(row.version)` (the ORM). **After:** `return int(_own(ctx, actor, _pid(ctx, target))["interaction_version"])`, where `_own` is `ctx.profiles.call(actor, "GET", f"{INTERACTIONS}/{target_profile_id}")` checked as `OwnInteractionVersions`. `_block_version()` likewise takes `block_version` from the read (it read `Block` before), and `_unmatch`'s default version comes from the match read (`_match_version`, `GET api/v1/matches/<id>`), which a participant has; it read `Match` before. The stale probe `match.version + 5` became the match read's version plus 5.
- **The assertion** that every expected version a case sends comes from a command's own answer or a read: `_Ledger` (in `interaction_cases.py`) wraps every case of the module (Stage B's 19 and B-C1's 7). It records each version served to each device (the read's two, and each command answer's or match read's version, by actor, family and object) and each direct call's result, through `ctx.profiles.call` and the four commands in `glow_discovery.interactions` that the cases call directly (the routes keep their own references); it fails the case if a command carried a version never served to it and the command was neither refused before its version is judged (401, 400, 403, 404, 405, `idempotency_conflict`) nor refused `stale_version` (the deliberate stale probes). Each case's detail line gains its count, for example `[15 expected versions sent, each from an answer or a read, or refused first or as stale (0)]`. The offline `ClientVersionTests` add the static form (the three helpers read routes, and no sent version, followed through its function's assignments, is a row's `.version` or a query) and the ledger's rules with a fake. (Corrected in B-C3, item 7: until B-C3 both wrappers recorded a command's own answer before checking the version it sent, so an answer that echoed that version vouched for it, B-C2's delta re-review's F6 and Codex's fifth finding; since B-C3 each command's version is checked against the versions served before it was sent, and only then is its answer recorded. A `stale_version` refusal caused by the batch or the pair, and a `not_found` made after the version check, still accept an unserved version: S5's remainder, a stated limit.)
- **The recovery cases** are discovery cases (`own_versions.recovery_*`), and D14's record runs over them as over every case (their statements are in the suite's `row locks sent (D14)` line).

### The contract (item 3)

- **`OwnInteractionVersions`** joins `production/gapp-api-v1.schema.json`'s `$defs` and, at the end, `AppResponse.data`'s `oneOf`; two `if`/`then`/`else` rules hold `none` to version 0 and every other state to 1 or more. `openapi.json` gains its component only (paths stay empty). No request DTO, no `ApiError` code, and no existing DTO's properties or enums change.
- **The registry:** F08's response contracts `["InteractionOutcome", "InteractionUndoOutcome", "OwnInteractionVersions"]` and F13's `["BlockOutcome", "OwnInteractionVersions"]`, each with `"OwnInteractionVersions": ["owner"]` in its projection roles; no request contract or transition changes. The allowed example (the owner) and the forbidden ones (the other member, a participant, a revoked session, staff, the system, a provider, the public: `not_found`) are in `test_sv3_own_versions_are_the_actors_own_and_additive`.
- **The corpus** gains 66 cases (9 valid, 57 invalid; 836 → 902), listed in the contracts README's "SV3 Stage B, correction B-C1".
- **Regeneration:** `npm run generate --prefix packages/contracts` rewrote `apps/mobile/src/contracts/generated/gapp-api-v1.ts` (the new type and the two `AppResponse` unions), `validators.d.ts` (`validateOwnInteractionVersions`) and `validators.js`; `gapp-dev-v1.ts` unchanged. A second run changed nothing (the working tree's diff checksum the same before and after).

### Local database runs (iteration, not evidence)

Under P06.DB's D4: a throwaway PostgreSQL from the CI job's image pinned by digest, on `127.0.0.1:5433` only, a generated superuser and role password and the discovery database's marker as its comment, kept in files in a scratch directory readable only by the session (the superuser password file made world-readable for the container's own user, on this throwaway only), none of `DATABASE_URL`, `HD_API_KEY`, `GEO_API_KEY` or any `PG*` name, migrations applied through `python -m django migrate` under the discovery settings, and the container and files removed afterwards. The Docker daemon was not running and was started for these runs; Docker Hub first answered 429 and the pull succeeded after a 20 s wait.

- **Verdict: changes required.** No blocking finding. Seven should-fix findings fall in correction classes: four need record or limit wording only, three a decision or a test change. Nine nits. It found no path that reveals a like: once the like's recheck has passed under both locks, every refusal of `activate_reciprocal` is unreachable (eligibility's consent rule is stricter than `_contact_state_checks`, and `VISIBLE`, `ACTIVE`, both blocks and a match row in any state imply its other checks), so no 409 shows the target's like. Idempotency, the recheck at commit, ownership, the canonical lock order and B-C1's read hold; the AST comparison holds for all 32 existing definitions in `contact.py` and its module. The Dev Manager's conditions are met in code (DM-60's D9 to D12, DM-68, DM-70's BB3, DM-71, DM-72's item 4, DM-73's EE1 to EE8, DM-74's FF1 to FF3 and FF6), with three gaps: D8's "a block's direction is never shown" (#1), DD5's "keeping what it protects" (#3) and BB3's "states it in Limits" (#6). So a projection showing the member who did not block another state (`unmatched`, say) would still differ from a real unmatch under that member's own unmatch, and under the blocker's later unmatch (a version advancing on a match that reads `unmatched`): hiding the direction is not a change to the projection alone.

  - #7: `MatchView.get` checks neither a body nor a content type, while the module docstring (lines 21-25) says every route refuses both.

### Undo, one step (item 2)

- **Before** (`470d97a`): `latest = DirectionalInteraction.objects.filter(actor_id=account_id, state="passed").order_by("-decided_at", "-id").values_list("id", flat=True).first()`.
- **After:** `latest = DirectionalInteraction.objects.filter(actor_id=account_id).order_by("-decided_at", "-id").values_list("id", flat=True).first()`. Nothing else in `undo` changed: the row must still be `passed` (`no_pass`), `latest != row.id` is still `state_conflict` with the internal reason `not_the_most_recent_pass` (kept), and the window check follows.
- **The case** (`settings.undo_off_and_on`) gains: after undo B, undo A is refused (409 `state_conflict`; the direct call names `not_the_most_recent_pass`) and writes nothing; after a like of B over its withdrawn row, undo A is still refused and writes nothing; every refusal its docstring names is counted as writing nothing; and its window check backdates the actor's later rows (the first pass 4 days, the second target's like 3 days) before the third pass (2 days), so that pass is the actor's latest decision and is refused for its window only (`undo_window_ended`).
- **The four other cases that call undo** (`interactions.logs_no_private_value`, `interactions.pointer_order`, `own_versions.each_state_and_version`, `own_versions.recovery_like_after_undo`) keep their verdicts (`PASS` locally and in the run of record).

### D14: the reading and the resolution (items 4 and 5)

- **The reading** (`row_locks._read`): a regex-driven PostgreSQL lexer, with `standard_conforming_strings` on (PostgreSQL's default, stated in the code). It drops every comment (`--` to a line end; `/* … */`, nested) and masks every string constant (`'…'` with `''`; `E'…'` with backslash escapes; `U&'…'`; dollar quotes, the tag empty or an identifier; a constant continued across a line end keeps its kind). A quoted identifier is kept as a name. Only PostgreSQL's whitespace separates tokens (any other character, and a `$` after it, continues an identifier), and names are cut to 63 bytes. **By rule:** an unterminated string constant, quoted identifier or block comment fails; a `DO` statement (at a statement's start, so `ON CONFLICT DO` is not one) fails; a `U&` quoted identifier fails with `FOR UPDATE` (see "Deviations and readings").
- **The resolution** (`row_locks._resolve`), for a statement that takes `FOR UPDATE` and names the account table (in any case): every `FROM`, `JOIN` and comma item at every depth is read, with its form; an item binds its alias where it has one (an unreserved keyword such as `set` can be one), and its table's name only where it has none, as PostgreSQL binds them; a `WITH` query's name and an `UPDATE`, `INTO` or `DELETE FROM` target are bindings too, none plain. An `OF` name must be bound once, in any scope, by a plain item (not a comma item, schema-qualified, `ONLY`, `*`, a sub-query, a function, a column alias list or `TABLESAMPLE`), whose table is a model's and not the account table. The account table may appear only as a plain item's table outside any sub-query, a column's qualifier, or a name in a lock clause's `OF` list (read as a reference, so `test_an_of_naming_only_other_tables_passes` keeps its verdict). A `FROM` after `IS DISTINCT` or among a function's arguments opens no list.
- **A fast path:** a statement with no single quote, comment, `$` or `&`, an even count of `"`, no possible `DO` and no writer's lock in its text cannot fail, and is not read further (about 12 µs on a 1.7 KB statement, against about 40 µs for `470d97a`'s rule); a full read is about 37 µs for a short account lock and about 4 ms for the 10 KB discovery predicates.
- **The counts** (`RowLockRecord.note`) now use the same reading as the verdict, so a lock or the account table's name inside a constant or a comment is not counted. Which statements are recorded, from which source, and the summary line's form are unchanged.

### The earlier tests (DD5; item 10)

- **`settings.undo_off_and_on`:** before, an older pass was refused only while a newer pass stood, and the window check backdated the first pass 3 days and the third 2 (after a like of the second at the current time). After: the one-step checks above; the window check backdates the first pass 4 days and the second target's like 3 days before the third pass's 2. Why: under the new rule the third pass would be refused for not being the latest decision before its window was checked (DM-75 4.2).
- **`settings.resurfacing_off_and_on`:** before, first pages only, one resurfaced candidate. After: two resurfaced candidates (8 and 9 days), a first page with `limit` 1 and a cursor, and the page fetched by cursor reaching the other one (#16); its existing checks stand.
- **`routes.interaction_refusals_and_ownership`:** before, "another member's batch" liked the batch's owner, and the docstring said every route refuses a type and a body. After: a fourth member, asserted to be an entry of the third member's batch, is liked by the second member naming that batch, with the two members' account versions asserted equal so only the batch-owner comparison can refuse it; the docstring says what each route refuses (GG22 and GG23's words) and that B-C1's read is checked by `own_versions.owner_only_and_held_for_share`; the detail line names the checks made on each route (#15, #7).
- **`block.works_after_unmatch_and_while_paused`:** its docstring (GG7) and one message (GG8); its assertions unchanged.
- **`test_the_plan_covers_stage_as_and_stage_bs_acceptance_checks`:** before, Stage A's labels pooled over every case. After: Stage A's checks and decisions over Stage A's own cases (neither list), none of 7 to 11 or B-C1 there; Stage B's over `STAGE_B_CASES`; B-C1's over `B_C1_CASES`, each naming B-C1.
- **`test_the_method_around_the_body_is_activate_matchs_with_one_import`:** it also compares the nested `body`'s decorators.
- **`test_row_locks_offline.py`:** only added to (the diff against `f39bd0d` removes no line); all 17 earlier tests keep their statements and verdicts.
- No other earlier test changed or failed. The case count stays 66.

### Local database runs (iteration, not evidence)

Under P06.DB's D4: PostgreSQL from the CI job's pinned image (`postgres@sha256:d74eeac9…`, pulled from Docker Hub after one 429), published on `127.0.0.1:5433` only; generated passwords, keys and the run's marker in a mode-700 directory; a fresh `glow_discovery_proof` with its marker as its comment for each run; no `DATABASE_URL`, `HD_API_KEY`, `GEO_API_KEY` or `PG*` name. ### An independent read before the push

Four read-only reviewers (subagents, no repository change, no database) read B-C2 at `581966e`: a hunt for statements PostgreSQL would lock `FOR UPDATE` on account rows that the rule passes; a hunt for false refusals of what the app and suites send; the changed cases' soundness; and completeness against the brief.

- **Found and fixed** (`fb201a4`): non-ASCII whitespace read as a token boundary before a dollar quote; an `E''` constant's continuation read as a standard string; unreserved keywords (`set`, `update`, `values`, `between`) as bare aliases unrecorded; names over 63 bytes not truncated; a `FROM` after `IS DISTINCT` read as a list. And a latent false refusal: an aliased item's table name bound, which would refuse Django's `select_for_update(of=("self",))` on a table joined to itself (now bound as PostgreSQL binds it). The continuation fix first missed the single-pass reading path; the recheck caught it.
- **Fixed** (`23ca206`, `4860b56`): `UndoSelectionTests` compared only the last `filter`, so a chained filter or `exclude` passed (it now compares the whole expression); the routes case's batch check rested on an unstated precondition (now asserted); the undo and resurfacing docstrings; a docstring line in `row_locks.py`.
- **No false refusal:** 583 SQL literals, 12 module-level SQL constants, 590 statements of Django's migration DDL and the ORM's lock forms all pass.
- **Completeness:** items 1 to 9 done in full; every changed path an owned path; the eighteen texts once each.

### Deviations, readings and what could not be run

- **The `U&` rule fires only with `FOR UPDATE`.** The brief and GG18 say a statement holding a `U&` quoted identifier fails; item 4 also says each planted text, the `U&` identifier among them, passes with `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`. The two hold together only if the rule is read with the lock rule's own condition, so the code fails it when the statement takes `FOR UPDATE` (where its escapes could spell the account table), and the docstring says so. The README keeps GG18's words.
- **Statements that fail by rule whatever their lock:** a `DO` statement (anywhere a statement starts) and an unterminated span; neither depends on `FOR UPDATE` (DM-75 6.2 (c): the `DO` statement "fails either way").
- **An item binds its alias, or its table's name only without one** (PostgreSQL's reading of "a table named in a plain `FROM` or `JOIN` item or that item's alias"). Binding both would refuse Django forms the suites could send, though PostgreSQL binds the name once.
- **The other binding forms** (`WITH` names, `UPDATE`, `INTO` and `DELETE FROM` targets, column alias lists, `TABLESAMPLE`) count as bindings and are never plain: more refusals, never fewer.
- **A name in a lock clause's `OF` list is read as a reference** (added in B-C3, item 2; B-C2's delta re-review, F2; DM-77 section 5): DM-75 6.2 (d), "the account table appears only as such an item's table or a column's qualifier", is read with that exception, as `_resolve` reads it. A name in an `OF` list refers to a binding and binds nothing; a `FOR UPDATE OF` name is still resolved by its binding and refused if that binding is the account table, and a `FOR SHARE OF` the account table takes only a reader's lock on its rows (PostgreSQL 17.11, the review's check), which `test_a_qualifier_and_a_reference_in_a_lock_clause_are_read_as_references` keeps passing.
- **`standard_conforming_strings`** is assumed on, PostgreSQL's default and the suites' setting; a session that turned it off would read `'\'` differently. Stated in the code.
- **A `CREATE FUNCTION` or `CREATE PROCEDURE` body** written as a string constant is now masked, so its text no longer fails the rule (`470d97a` refused such a body with a `FOR UPDATE` naming the account table); the suites' own-objects check still fails any function, procedure, view or rule that exists when it runs, but not one created and dropped inside a run. The brief names only `DO` (DM-75 6.2 (c)), so B-C2 adds no rule for it; for the delta re-review or the Dev Manager. No case creates one.
- **The block case's detail line** still ends "hidden" (unchanged); item 1 names only its docstring and one message.
- **The proof README's SV3 paragraph** also names DM-75 where it named DM-73 as the last condition, and gains a sentence on B-C2, beside item 8's counts and revision.
- **Not run:** the mobile app's checks, the API artifact and Stream jobs and the ordering, identity and profile suites locally (CI's run of record runs them); no provider, HDE or `proofs/stream-chat` command.

## PR47 at `b0386f8`, and Codex's fifth finding

App Manager 11, 16 November 2026 (01:22 UTC).

- **PR47 at `b0386f8`** (B-C2 merged, its verification and the delta re-review prompt's revision 1): its push run 43783914346 and pull-request run 43784064946 passed. Codex's code review of `b0386f8` completed with no new comment; its state counts the findings still unresolved.
- **Codex's fifth finding** (P2, on `e6fa3c1`, posted at 23:23 UTC on 15 November; [thread](https://github.com/amthorn78/glow-dating-app/pull/47#discussion_r4580718359)), on `proofs/postgres-ordering/glow_ordering_proof/interaction_cases.py` line 2073:
  - B-C1's `_as_a_client` records a command's own answer (`_Ledger.served_from`) before it checks the version the command sent (`_Ledger.check`), in its routes' wrapper (lines 2069 to 2073 at `4860b56`) and its direct one (2080 to 2081);
  - so a command that answers with the version it was sent (an unmatch of a match already `unmatched`, `no_change`, for one) satisfies the ledger by its own answer, even when the version came from the database.
- **The manager's check:** confirmed in the code at `4860b56`. It is B-C1's code; B-C2 did not change it (`git diff 470d97a 4860b56` touches neither call). It weakens the proof of FF3's rule that every version a case sends comes from a command's own answer or the read, a test that can pass where it should fail; the app is not affected.
- **Disposition:** the delta re-review prompt, revised to revision 2 before it was given, adds it to the review's scope (focus 9). Its fix goes into the correction that follows the review, with the review's findings, as Dev Manager 5 rules. The manager read the finding only at 01:20, after writing revision 1 (AM11-29).

USING` (or `MERGE ... USING`) item. The control, both bindings plain, is refused. No account row is reachable this way (placing the account table in such a form trips the appearance check), but the rule's words name the form; PostgreSQL resolves `OF x` to the first range-table entry named `x`, even one hidden by a join alias | `row_locks.py:557-558`; the scan loop at `:609-653` |

No case in run 43780988590 relies on it | `interaction_cases.py:2069` before `:2073`; `:2080` before `:2081` |

- **Stated limits** (none a correction class; each with its planted example in the report):
  - **S1:** a `CREATE FUNCTION` or `CREATE PROCEDURE` body that is a string constant (dollar-quoted, `'...'` or `E'...'`) and takes `FOR UPDATE` on the account table, and a `dblink` query argument: refused at `470d97a`, passing at `4860b56` (class 3's exception: item 4 masks every string constant and excepts only `DO`). Planted on 17.11 as a temporary function, it held the account row `FOR UPDATE` with every statement passing and the own-objects counts 0, 0, 0 (F3). DM-75 6.2 (c)'s reason for the `DO` rule applies word for word.
  - **S2:** a placeholder next to `$` becomes a dollar-quote delimiter only after psycopg binds it: `cursor.execute("SELECT id, $q%s$ $q1$ FROM glow_persistence_appaccount FOR UPDATE --$q1$", [1])`, through Django's cursor with the record installed, held the account row `FOR UPDATE` while D14 printed `PASS` (`470d97a`: `FAIL`). Nothing sends it today.
  - **S3:** a session with `standard_conforming_strings = off` (`'\'` then `' FROM ... FOR UPDATE --'`): refused at `470d97a`, passing at `4860b56`; the code and this record state it, the README does not.
  - **S4:** a placeholder against its lock clause (`= %sFOR UPDATE`) and SQL inside a parameter's value written into a span: passing at both heads (not a regression).
  - **S5:** in the ledger, after the simple fix, a concurrent direct answer could still vouch for a command, and a `stale_version` refusal caused by the batch or pair, or a `not_found` after the version check, accepts an unserved version. No case is affected; a snapshot taken before the send closes the first.
- **Nits:** N1, this record's 54-row planted-text table spliced into "`TABLESAMPLE`" twice (lines 1932 and 2130, at `423f34a`), breaking "The resolution" bullet and "The other binding forms" deviation; N2, GG21's "its connections are about twice its threads" is false in the run of record (discovery 64 connections in 3 threads, profile 26 in 5); N3, GG25's note on migration 0011 omits that 0011 as written backfills one instant and validates its constraints at once (the P11 plan line 165); N4, "`_Ledger` checks it for every command" overstates by F6 (`README.md:19`; this record line 1766); N5, the block case's detail line still ends "hidden" (`interaction_cases.py:1106`, disclosed); N6, loose wording in `row_locks.py:35-37` and `:47-50`; N7, with `--` removed from `_SPAN_MARKS` all 346 offline tests pass while `FOR -- c` then newline `UPDATE` on the account table passes, so the fast-path agreement test needs a `--` statement (`test_row_locks_offline.py:310`); N8, `UndoSelectionTests` pins only the `if` line, so removing undo's `raise` passes all 628 API tests (`settings.undo_off_and_on` fails it on a database, 65/66); N9, `_resolve` is quadratic in parenthesis depth (5,000 levels take 1.2 s); N10, the fast path assumes PostgreSQL 15 or later (`1FOR UPDATE` is trailing junk in 17, a lock in 14).
- **Focus points answered:** no lexical bypass with no parameters and default settings (200 hand-built texts and 290,000 random fragments against PostgreSQL 17.11 in the pinned image; the oracle: a second connection's `FOR KEY SHARE NOWAIT` fails only under a held `FOR UPDATE`); the resolution's named forms each fail, and 20,803 oracle statements found none passing under a `FOR UPDATE` on an account row, F5 apart; 15 texts refused at `470d97a` pass now, ten of them S1's bodies and five constant-only texts that take no lock; `470d97a`'s 17 row-lock tests pass at `4860b56`; the lock scan is byte-identical at both heads (52 calls, 0 joining, 7 account locks, none other than `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`); 74 lock statements compiled from the suites' forms give no difference in reason, writer, account or `for_update`; undo selects only the actor's latest decision of any state, read under the actor's lock in the same transaction, with ties broken by `-id` (no app write can tie: `clock_timestamp()` is read under the lock); the plan-coverage test, the routes precondition, resurfacing's cutoff and the decorator test each fail under their mutation; DM-75's eighteen texts are each found once, byte-exact; 13 paths changed, all within GG27.
- **Codex's threads:** #2 (r4580670729), #4 (r4579396514) and #5 (r4580670735) are answered at `4860b56` (each text refused, its `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` variant passing); #1 is carried to SV4; the fifth (r4580718359) is not answered (F6). All five are still unresolved on GitHub.

### D14: the rules, the bindings and the filter (items 1 to 6)

- **Item 1 (F1)** and **item 2 (F2):** words only (II1, II2); the code is unchanged for them. Item 2's reading is added to B-C2's "Deviations, readings and what could not be run" above.
- **The creation rule (item 3, S1):** `judge` refuses a statement whose tokens, as `_read` gives them (comments dropped, constants masked, quoted identifiers kept as names), hold `CREATE`, then optionally `OR REPLACE`, then `FUNCTION` or `PROCEDURE`, whatever its lock (reason `CREATES_ROUTINE`), checked after the `DO` rule. The fast path no longer passes unread a statement whose text holds the words `CREATE` and `FUNCTION` or `PROCEDURE`, so a SQL-standard body (`BEGIN ATOMIC`), which holds no constant, is read and refused.
- **The filter (item 3, F3):** each of `OWN_OBJECTS`'s three counts now excludes only the schemas `pg_catalog`, `information_schema` and `pg_toast`, and those matching `pg\_toast\_temp\_%`; the `pg\_%` filter, which excluded every temporary schema (`pg_temp_N`), is gone. The functions' count still leaves out an extension's own functions (`pg_depend`, `deptype = 'e'`), as before; nothing else in the query changed.
- **The bindings (item 5, F5):** in `_resolve`, a parenthesis that stands where a `FROM` item does and holds no `SELECT`, `VALUES`, `TABLE` or `WITH` directly inside it (and more than one token, so `ONLY (name)` is read as before) is a parenthesised join: its items, nested ones included, are read as a `FROM` list's, every one a binding and none plain. A `USING` after a `DELETE` or `MERGE` at the same depth, with no `JOIN` between them, opens a list whose every item (its `JOIN` items included) is a binding, none plain. A `USING` after a `JOIN` is that join's column list and binds nothing. Which parentheses directly hold a sub-query is computed once per statement, so the resolver's time on deep nesting is as at `4860b56` (5,000 nested levels: 1.01 s at the head, 0.99 s at `4860b56`; N9).
- **The placeholder rule (item 6, S2):** before everything else, `judge` refuses a statement (reason `GLUED_PLACEHOLDER`) whose text holds the word `UPDATE` (`\bUPDATE\b`, any case, anywhere, spans and comments included) and a placeholder (`%s`, `%b`, `%t`, or `%(name)s`, `%(name)b`, `%(name)t`, a name read as any characters but parentheses, so a name holding `(` was not read: B-C3's delta re-review, F1, corrected in B-C4) with a name character (a letter, digit, underscore, `$` or non-ASCII character) directly before or after it; `%%` is read as a literal percent sign and skipped. The prompt's readings of "the word" and of `%%` are the manager's, applied as given.
- **The docstring:** F4's qualification (lines 27-28 and `judge`'s docstring, the README's words), the two new rules, N6 (the whitespace sentence, now saying that every non-ASCII character is an identifier character and a `$` after one continues the identifier; and the rule's cannot-read list, now pointing to the resolution instead of its loose list), the resolution's new binding forms, II11, S3 and S4 in "What it does not see", and II5's words on the counts.

The controls keep their verdicts.

### The ledger (item 7, F6)

- **Before** (`4860b56`): the routes' wrapper called the route, recorded the reply's version (`_Ledger.served_from`), then checked the command (`_Ledger.check`) against everything served so far, its own answer included; the direct wrapper did the same (lines 2069 and 2073; 2080 and 2081).
- **After:** each wrapper takes `_Ledger.snapshot()` (a copy of the versions served so far) before sending a command, checks the command against that snapshot (`check`'s new optional `served` argument), and only then records the command's answer. So an answer echoing the version sent never vouches for it, and neither does a version another thread is served while the command is in flight (S5's first part). `check` without `served` reads the versions served so far, as before, so the existing ledger test's calls are unchanged. The wrapper is still `_as_a_client.<locals>.wrapped`.
- **S5's remainder** is stated in the README's "Limits": a `stale_version` refusal caused by the batch or the pair, and a `not_found` made after the version check, each accept an unserved version.

### Deviations, readings and what could not be run

- **`_Ledger` itself changed, beside the two wrappers.** Item 7's "against the versions served before the command was sent" needs the check to take a snapshot: `_Ledger` gains `snapshot()` and `check` an optional `served` argument (the prompt's stop-rule list anticipates a change to `check`'s signature). With `served` omitted it behaves as before, so `test_the_ledger_passes_only_a_served_version_or_a_refusal_first` is unchanged. `_as_a_client`'s own docstring says what it now does; `interaction_cases.py`'s module docstring, outside B-C3's owned lines, still says `_Ledger` "asserts it for every case", which remains true of what it asserts.
- **`OWN_OBJECTS`: only the schema filter.** Views and rules are still counted whatever extension owns them, and functions still leave out an extension's own, as at `4860b56`; the brief's "and extensions" is read as that existing handling, since B-C3's owned item is the schema filter.
- **The creation rule reads the words anywhere in the token stream,** not only at a statement's start (more refusals, never fewer; `create` is a reserved word, so it is never a bare name), and the fast path sends any text holding `CREATE` with `FUNCTION` or `PROCEDURE` to the full reading.
- **A parenthesised join** is recognised by what its parenthesis directly holds; a `USING` list's `JOIN` items are read as not plain too (an item of the list, read fail-closed). Both readings refuse more, never less.
- **S4 stated as it now stands:** its first form, a placeholder against its lock clause (`= %sFOR UPDATE`), is refused since B-C3 by item 6's rule, which the README says; what remains a limit is SQL inside a parameter's value, with a planted example checked on PostgreSQL.
- **The docstring also takes II5's words** on the counts (the README's words, as B-C2's item 6 asks of the docstring), and states S3 and S4 among what it does not see.
- **The README's SV3 paragraph:** besides revision 18 and N4, it names DM-77 as its last condition, its Stage B sentence's own-objects words now say what the check counts, and it gains a B-C3 sentence.
- **N1** was a script's replacement of the word `TABLE` in "`TABLESAMPLE`" by the planted-text table; both sentences were restored by replacing each spliced copy with `TABLE`, and the table is kept once, after the script note in "D14: the reading and the resolution".
- **N4** in this record is a correction note appended to B-C1's "The assertion" bullet; its earlier words are kept.
- **Codex's addition on `b67d9e3`** (P2, "Reject executable routine definitions before masking their bodies") is planted as `CODEX_SEQUENCE`: the `CREATE FUNCTION public.d14_lock() … PERFORM id FROM glow_persistence_appaccount FOR UPDATE …` is refused at the head and passes at `4860b56`; its call and `DROP FUNCTION` pass at both. The thread is the manager's to resolve.
- **A reversal's first pass** passed on a stale bytecode cache (the `%%` control); by hand it failed, and the script was changed to clear caches and write no bytecode before every reversal was run again, each failing.
- **The first local discovery run's log file** was deleted when the second run's setup recreated its scratch directory; its result lines are quoted above, as read when it ended.
- **Docker:** the daemon was not running; it was started for the D4 runs and stopped afterwards.
- **Not run:** the ordering, identity and profile suites locally (the run of record runs them); the mobile and Stream jobs locally; no provider, HDE or `proofs/stream-chat` command.

| # | Severity | Class | What | Where (at `edc883e`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Should fix | Class 2 (item 6) | A named placeholder whose name holds `(` is not read as a placeholder: the rule reads `%(` + `[^()]*` + `)`, while psycopg 3.3.6 reads any character but `)` in a name. `SELECT id, $q%((n)s$ $q1$ FROM glow_persistence_appaccount FOR UPDATE --$q1$` with `{"(n": 1}` (and `%(a(b)s`) passes at both heads; psycopg binds it to S2's text, and PostgreSQL 17.11 held the account row `FOR UPDATE`. The control `%(n)s` is refused | `row_locks.py:170` (`_PLACEHOLDER`); this record's line 2207 |
| F2 | Should fix | Class 2 ("a name bound twice", as F5 was) | A `MERGE INTO` target's alias written without `AS` is not read as a binding: the `WITH … MERGE INTO glow_persistence_block x … FOR UPDATE OF x` form passes at both heads, with `AS x` refused. It takes no account lock on 17.11 | `row_locks.py:779` |
| F3 | Should fix | Class 2 (the same) | A join's `USING (…) AS x` alias (PostgreSQL 14 and later) binds `x`, and the rule skips it; the `WITH … JOIN … USING (id) AS x … FOR UPDATE OF x` form passes at both heads. It takes no account lock on 17.11 | `row_locks.py:741-746` |
| F4 | Should fix | Class 5 (DM-77's II4 and its section 6's premise), or a rule change for the Dev Manager | "Limits" says only an object created through a path the record does not see, and gone before the check, escapes. A view created through a path it does see escapes too: `CREATE TEMP VIEW v AS SELECT id FROM glow_persistence_appaccount`, then `SELECT id FROM v FOR UPDATE`, then `DROP VIEW v`, each pass at both heads (the locking statement names no account table, and `judge` passes it), and on 17.11 the non-superuser role held the account row `FOR UPDATE`, the counts then 0, 0, 0. The same with a `public` view and `FOR UPDATE OF v2`, and with `PREPARE … FOR UPDATE` then `EXECUTE`. Not a regression | `README.md:240`; `row_locks.py:568` |
| F5 | Should fix | Class 5 | S4's "passes … when the text itself holds no word `UPDATE`" implies the rule refuses the form when the text holds `UPDATE`; it does not, since a quote beside a placeholder is neither a name character nor `$`. `… WHERE state <> '%s' FOR NO KEY UPDATE` with the value `FOR UPDATE --` passes at the head; psycopg writes `''FOR UPDATE --''`, and 17.11 held the row `FOR UPDATE` | `README.md:241` |
| N1 | Nit | Test gap (as N7, N8) | No test text has `$` as a placeholder's only glued neighbour; with `$` removed from `_NAME_CHARACTER` all 37 row-lock tests pass, while `SELECT id, $%s$ $NULL$ … FOR UPDATE --$NULL$` with `[None]` then passes (17.11 holds `FOR UPDATE`) | `test_row_locks_offline.py:508` |
| N2 | Nit | Test gap | A `JOIN` item inside a `USING` list is read as not plain only by the final pass, which no test pins (it takes no lock) | `row_locks.py:794` |
| N3 | Nit | Record nit (docstring) | "What it does not see" still lists "a placeholder against its lock clause", which the rule now refuses (an under-claim) | `row_locks.py:99` |

### D14: the placeholder reading, the span rule, the view-creation rule, the relations check and the bindings (items 1 to 5)

- **The placeholder reading (item 1, F1):** `_PLACEHOLDER` is `%%|%\([^)]+\)[sbt]|%[sbt]`, matched over the whole text in order, as psycopg 3.3.6's `_re_placeholder` scans it: `%s`, `%b` or `%t`, or a name of one or more characters other than `)` in parentheses followed by one of them; `%%` is a literal percent sign and skipped. Both placeholder tests read placeholders through one function (`_placeholders`). Where psycopg sends a statement with parameters, every `%` it reads is one of these three forms (any other raises before sending), and on such texts this pattern reads the same placeholders; a test (`test_the_placeholders_are_those_psycopg_reads`) checks the count and names against psycopg's own `_split_query`.
- **The span rule (item 2, F5):** after the glued-placeholder test and before the fast path, a statement that holds a placeholder and a span mark (`'`, `"`, `$`, `--` or `/*`) is checked: if any part of a placeholder lies inside a string constant (`'…'`, `E'…'`, `U&'…'`, dollar-quoted, a continued constant whole), a quoted identifier (`"…"`, `U&"…"`) or a comment (`--`, `/* … */`), the statement fails (`PLACEHOLDER_IN_SPAN`), whatever its lock or text. The spans' extents come from `_read`, which now records each span's start and end when asked; an unterminated span fails as before. A text whose only span mark is `"`, with an even count of them (every statement Django writes), is judged without reading it in full: such a text holds only ended quoted identifiers, so a `%` lies inside one exactly where an odd count of double quotes stands before it, and a placeholder holding a `"` overlaps one. A fuzz of 146,871 such texts gave the same verdict by this path and by `_read`'s spans, and a test pins the two to agree.
- **The view-creation rule (item 3, F4):** `_creates` (formerly `_creates_routine`) refuses, on tokens, `CREATE [OR REPLACE] FUNCTION` or `PROCEDURE` as before (`CREATES_ROUTINE`), and `CREATE [OR REPLACE]` followed by any of `TEMP`, `TEMPORARY`, `LOCAL`, `GLOBAL`, `UNLOGGED`, `RECURSIVE` and `MATERIALIZED`, then `VIEW` (`CREATES_VIEW`), wherever a `CREATE` stands (after a `;`, or in `CREATE SCHEMA … CREATE VIEW`). The fast path's creation test reads `VIEW` as it reads `FUNCTION` and `PROCEDURE` (`_MAYBE_CREATED`).
- **The relations check (item 3, Codex on `4c2f918`):** with the models' tables given, a statement that takes `FOR UPDATE` and does not name the account table goes to `_relations`: a `TABLE` word anywhere fails it (`TABLE_COMMAND`); parentheses it cannot read fail it; otherwise every item `_resolve`'s reading binds that has a relation name (each `FROM`, `JOIN`, comma, `USING`-list, `INTO`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE FROM` item, in any scope, and each `WITH` query's name) must be one of the tables, written without a schema (`OUTSIDE_THE_MODELS`); an item after a word that the reading takes as the end of the `FROM` list is not read, a stated limit under the freeze whose mechanism the proof README's "Limits" states (B-C4's delta re-review, F1, and B-C5's short review, R1; DM-79, DM-80). A sub-query's and a function's items carry no relation name; their own items are read in turn. Without the models' tables (before Django's registry is ready) the check does not apply. `_resolve` was split for it into `_bindings` (the reading, unchanged but for item 4 and two recorded facts: whether an item's name is schema-qualified, and a `WITH` query's name) and `_account_rule` (the account rule, unchanged).
- **The bindings (item 4, F2, F3):** a `MERGE INTO` target reads an alias as a `FROM` item does (`USING`, a reserved word, ends it); `INSERT … INTO` and `SELECT … INTO` still read one only after `AS`. A join's `USING (…)` followed by `AS name` binds that name to an item with no table, not plain, and the scan steps over the two tokens. `excluded` is not read (JJ9).
- **The nits (item 5):** N1 and N2 are tests (below); N3 is JJ10.
- **The docstring:** the reading paragraph (the view rule, the placeholder reading and the two placeholder tests), the rule paragraph (the relations check), the resolution paragraph (the two new bindings), the own-objects paragraph (a view's creation) and the fast-path sentence (`VIEW`) say what the README says, in this session's words; "What it does not see" is JJ10.

The review's texts each pass at `edc883e` and are refused at the head: F1's two, F5's (with and without its lock clause), each other span form and a name holding a quote, F4's negative control (the creation and the lock; the `DROP VIEW` passes at both), the `public` view and `PREPARE`, and F2's and F3's forms. The review gave F2's and F3's texts in elided form (`WITH … MERGE INTO glow_persistence_block x … FOR UPDATE OF x`, `WITH … JOIN … USING (id) AS x … FOR UPDATE OF x`); the texts here are this session's, built to those forms and checked to pass at `edc883e`. N1's and N2's texts are refused at both heads: they pin rules `edc883e` already had, and their reversals are the evidence. The controls keep their verdicts.

**The stated limits' examples** (JJ6), planted with the head's `refusal`, the models' tables given: `CREATE TEMP VIEW glow_persistence_match AS SELECT id FROM glow_persistence_appaccount` is refused (a view's creation), and `SELECT id FROM glow_persistence_match ORDER BY id LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE` then passes; `ALTER TABLE glow_persistence_match RENAME TO glow_scratch_old`, `ALTER TABLE glow_persistence_appaccount RENAME TO glow_persistence_match` and `SELECT id FROM glow_persistence_match FOR UPDATE` each pass; `ALTER TABLE glow_persistence_appaccount INHERIT glow_persistence_block` and `SELECT id FROM glow_persistence_block FOR UPDATE` each pass; without the models' tables (before Django's registry is ready), `SELECT id FROM v FOR UPDATE` passes. On the local PostgreSQL 17.11 below (a superuser session; informational), a `FOR UPDATE` through a temporary view named `glow_persistence_match` over the account table held the account row (another session's `FOR KEY SHARE NOWAIT` failed: `could not obtain lock on row in relation "glow_persistence_appaccount"`), and with `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` it did not. DM-78 had reasoned this from PostgreSQL's search order without running it.

### The tests and their reversals (D18)

Every reversal was a temporary edit of `row_locks.py` applied by script (the old text found exactly once), the named tests run under `env -i` with bytecode writing off and every `__pycache__` cleared before and after, the file restored and checked byte-equal. Seventeen new tests in `tests/test_row_locks_offline.py` (37 to 54, all passing; the proof's offline suite 359 to 376), and each test's statements judged at `edc883e` in the table above. The head's test module run against `edc883e`'s code: `Ran 54 tests`, `FAILED (failures=26, errors=32)` (the errors are the names `edc883e` lacks: `CREATES_VIEW`, `PLACEHOLDER_IN_SPAN`, `_placeholders` and the like).

### The freeze

From this head (`07d8de3`), D14's rules are frozen ("B-C4's review, and how D14's line ends"): they change only to correct a class 1 defect or a regression, or by a design the Dev Manager reads; any other form a rule does not see is a stated limit.

### Deviations, readings and what could not be run

- **The view words, read wider than the brief's list.** The brief names `CREATE [OR REPLACE] [TEMP | TEMPORARY] [RECURSIVE] VIEW` and `CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW`, "the words read as PostgreSQL reads them". PostgreSQL's grammar also reads `LOCAL` or `GLOBAL` before `TEMP`/`TEMPORARY`, and `UNLOGGED` (an error for a view, accepted for a materialized view), between `CREATE` and `VIEW`; `_creates` skips any run of those seven words, so each such form is refused too (more refusals, never fewer; `CREATE LOCAL TEMPORARY VIEW` and `CREATE UNLOGGED MATERIALIZED VIEW IF NOT EXISTS` are planted). The docstring says so; the README carries DM-78's words as given.
- **A `TABLE` command is read as the word `TABLE` anywhere** in a statement the relations check reads (a reserved word, never a bare name), so `CREATE TABLE` or `LOCK TABLE` text in such a statement fails it too (fail-closed).
- **The quoted-identifier path** in the span test is this session's, for the run's duration: the first local run with every Django statement read in full took `judge` from about 31 µs to 221 µs a statement. It is exact for the texts it takes (argued in the code's comment, fuzzed against `_read`'s spans, pinned by a test with its reversal); every other text is read by `_read`.
- **`%()s` (an empty name)** was a placeholder to `edc883e`'s pattern and is not to psycopg 3.3.6 or to B-C4's: psycopg reads `%(` and raises "incomplete placeholder" before sending, so such a text never reaches PostgreSQL with its parameters. It is not the only change in that direction: where a name holds `%(`, `edc883e`'s pattern read the inner placeholder (`%(b)s` in `%(a%(b)s`), where B-C4's reads one placeholder named `a%(b`, as psycopg does, so `UPDATE glow_persistence_match SET version = %(a%(b)s WHERE id = 1`, refused at `edc883e` as a glued placeholder, passes at B-C4's head (B-C4's delta re-review, F3 and N1; DM-79).
- **Item 3's database reversal** reads F4's three statements as planted on Django's cursor in `row_locks.driver_forms_are_seen`, with the case's own-objects read after the drop, as B-C3's item 3 planted its objects; the oracle check of the stated limit ran as the container's superuser over its socket (no role password in any process), not as the proof's role.
- **R7 at first passed:** no statement isolated a `WITH` query's name (each `WITH` form also read a relation outside the models). A statement whose only such name is an unused `WITH q` was added, and R7 then failed; every reversal was run again on the final code, each failing.
- **F2's and F3's texts** are this session's, built to the review's elided forms (above).
- **Codex's thread on `cbea3ca`** (r4588443647, the wrapper-level negative control) is answered by `test_a_record_refuses_f5s_text_through_note`, which fails at `edc883e` (`0 != 1`) and under R2; Codex's thread on `4c2f918` (r4587204267) by item 3, and S2's (r4581883081) by item 1. The threads are the manager's to resolve.
- **Not run:** the ordering, identity and profile suites locally (the run of record runs them); the mobile and Stream jobs locally; no provider, HDE or `proofs/stream-chat` command.

## Delta re-review of B-C4 (code head `07d8de3`, against `edc883e`; the last of D14 under all five classes)

- **The session:** B-C4's bounded delta re-review, from the review prompt's revision 1 at `9fbb35d` (TypeSafe v8.2: Opus 5.5 at extra high; Nathan's pick not recorded). Relayed by Nathan on 16 November. - **Gates:** environment (names only: none of `DATABASE_URL`, `HD_API_KEY`, `GEO_API_KEY` or any `STREAM_*`, `PG*`, `R2_*`, `CLOUDFLARE_*` or `CF_*` name; the three AWS names present, not read, passed to nothing), start gate (one merge base `947576d`; `9fbb35d` byte-identical to `07d8de3` outside Markdown; no code after it; no review section here) and classification (`"full": true`). Toolchain CPython 3.12.14, Docker 29.9.2.
- **Size rule:** B-C4's non-Markdown delta from `edc883e` is `row_locks.py` and its offline test only, with no line in `services/api` and no earlier test line removed. The bounded review applied.
- **Verdict: changes required, by one class 5 finding that is record-only.** No class 1, 3 or 4 finding. Two forms D14 passes held an account row `FOR UPDATE` on a throwaway PostgreSQL: one class 2 (F1) and one outside every rule (F2); under the freeze (DM-78), both are stated limits. No statement a suite sends is refused or judged differently.

| # | Severity | Class | What | Where (at `07d8de3`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Should fix, as a limit | Class 2, a stated limit under the freeze | A word in `_ENDS_FROM` that is a valid alias (`set`, `values`), used bare at the `FROM` list's depth, closes the list, so a later `JOIN` or comma item is not read and the relations check passes it, `pg_temp.v` included. `SELECT m.id FROM glow_persistence_match m JOIN glow_persistence_block set ON set.id = m.id JOIN v ON true FOR UPDATE` (and `…, v FOR UPDATE`) passes with the models' tables given; the control with the alias `s` is refused. ### DM-80's seven pairs

Applied in the order LL1 to LL7 by one script (`apply_dm80.py`, in the session's scratchpad, not committed; its text below) that reads each pair from DM-80's report's section 9 at the start (the content of each quoted code block, without the `> ` markers; never retyped), checks the old passage once before and the new text once after, and stops at the first miss. It ran from the repository root under `env -i` with `python3.12 -I`, and exited 0.

| Pair | Target | Old passage, before | New text, before | New text, after | Old passage, after |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LL1 | `proofs/postgres-ordering/README.md` (the SV3 paragraph) | 1 | 0 | 1 | 1 (the new text keeps the old passage and adds B-C6's sentence after it, as DM-80 gives it) |
| LL2 | `proofs/postgres-ordering/README.md` (D14's entry) | 1 | 0 | 1 | 0 |
| LL3 | `proofs/postgres-ordering/README.md` (the limits' heading) | 1 | 0 | 1 | 0 |
| LL4 | `proofs/postgres-ordering/README.md` (F1's limit) | 1 | 0 | 1 | 0 |
| LL5 | `proofs/postgres-ordering/glow_ordering_proof/row_locks.py` (the module docstring) | 1 | 0 | 1 | 0 |
| LL6 | `row_locks.py` (`_relations`'s docstring) | 1 | 0 | 1 | 0 |
| LL7 | this record (the relations check's account) | 1 | 0 | 1 | 0 |

LL8 to LL11 were already applied by the manager and were not touched. LL1's new text holds B-C6's sentence for the README's SV3 paragraph; no other was written.

The script's output:

```
LL1 proofs/postgres-ordering/README.md: old before 1, new before 0, new after 1, old after 1
LL2 proofs/postgres-ordering/README.md: old before 1, new before 0, new after 1, old after 0
LL3 proofs/postgres-ordering/README.md: old before 1, new before 0, new after 1, old after 0
LL4 proofs/postgres-ordering/README.md: old before 1, new before 0, new after 1, old after 0
LL5 proofs/postgres-ordering/glow_ordering_proof/row_locks.py: old before 1, new before 0, new after 1, old after 0
LL6 proofs/postgres-ordering/glow_ordering_proof/row_locks.py: old before 1, new before 0, new after 1, old after 0
LL7 docs/testing/evidence/2026-11-11-sv3-eligibility-discovery-interactions.md: old before 1, new before 0, new after 1, old after 0
```

The script:

```python
"""Apply DM-80's LL1 to LL7 from its report's section 9, in order; never retyped."""
import re, sys
REPORT = "docs/continuity/dev-manager/reviews/2026-11-16-dm-80-sv3-b-c5-review-dispositions.md"
TARGETS = {
    "LL1": "proofs/postgres-ordering/README.md",
    "LL2": "proofs/postgres-ordering/README.md",
    "LL3": "proofs/postgres-ordering/README.md",
    "LL4": "proofs/postgres-ordering/README.md",
    "LL5": "proofs/postgres-ordering/glow_ordering_proof/row_locks.py",
    "LL6": "proofs/postgres-ordering/glow_ordering_proof/row_locks.py",
    "LL7": "docs/testing/evidence/2026-11-11-sv3-eligibility-discovery-interactions.md",
}
text = open(REPORT, encoding="utf-8").read()
sec9 = text.split("\n## 9. The words\n", 1)[1].split("\n## 10.", 1)[0]
parts = re.split(r"^\*\*(LL\d+) — ", sec9, flags=re.M)
pairs = {}
for k in range(1, len(parts), 2):
    blocks = []
    cur = None
    for line in parts[k + 1].split("\n"):
        if line == "> ```":
            if cur is None:
                cur = []
            else:
                blocks.append("\n".join(cur)); cur = None
        elif cur is not None:
            assert line.startswith("> ") or line == ">", repr(line)
            cur.append(line[2:] if line.startswith("> ") else "")
    assert len(blocks) == 2, (parts[k], len(blocks))
    pairs[parts[k]] = blocks
for name, path in TARGETS.items():
    old, new = pairs[name]
    body = open(path, encoding="utf-8").read()
    ob, nb = body.count(old), body.count(new)
    if ob != 1:
        print(f"{name} {path}: old passage found {ob} times before; STOP"); sys.exit(1)
    body = body.replace(old, new, 1)
    oa, na = body.count(old), body.count(new)
    print(f"{name} {path}: old before {ob}, new before {nb}, new after {na}, old after {oa}")
    if na != 1:
        print(f"{name}: new text found {na} times after; STOP"); sys.exit(1)
    open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(body)
```
